Grep for a claim's homes; do not count them from memory.) **The marker carries the guard the file's absence used to carry**, which review caught the first version dropping: written unconditionally with `cells=` derived from `ls "$OUT"/*.json`, the body could not disagree with the directory it sat in, so an arm that failed wholesale — host down, wrong port or auth, or the cohort mismatch this README's *"standalone gold is unremappable"* note describes — wrote `cells=0` and `compare_arms.py` read it as a clean, empty A/B: *"cells compared: 0, class flips: 0"*, exit 0. Reproduced end to end against a closed port, 14 cells NOT-PROMOTED. So the count recorded is the INTENDED one (the sibling's meaning, from what the loops actually fired rather than a hand-kept tally) beside how many landed, a run that fires cells and lands **none** writes no marker and exits 1, an invocation that would fire neither tier is refused before it starts, and any marker ALREADY in the directory is deleted immediately before the first cell is fired — BELOW both refusals, because a run refused before it fires changes nothing in the directory and so has nothing to fail closed about, while at the top of the file it destroyed a complete arm's marker on a caller error (a mistyped `CAPTURE_TIER_B`, or `CAPTURE_PATIENTS=none` with Tier B off), which `compare_arms.py` then exits 3 on until every cell is re-fired. That last one is what makes the other two an invariant rather than half of one: the refusal declines to WRITE a marker, but a re-capture into a non-empty directory — the script's own documented Tier-B resume, or the same arm re-fired after a GP swap — inherited the PREVIOUS run's marker, and `fire()` deliberately keeps the previous run's good cells, so a wholesale failure left the directory byte-for-byte the earlier run plus a marker asserting completeness and `compare_arms.py` (which reads the marker's presence, never its body) scored the old wording's answers as the new arm's: *"class flips: 0"*, exit 0. Reproduced against a closed port on a directory seeded with a previous run's cells and marker, and re-run after the fix, where the marker is gone and `compare_arms.py` exits 3. The guard is put in the WRITER because that is where an empty capture is knowable — and it is now in BOTH writers. `capture_probe_safety.sh` had the identical fail-open for its own family and was left alone for one round, which made this sentence describe a guard that was only half there: it wrote `cells=` from the MATRIX SIZE unconditionally, so an arm whose context fetches succeeded and whose `/search` cells all failed — a wedged or 500ing LLM, the state that script's own *"WARM THE LLAMA FIRST"* note warns about — left a marker asserting 20 cells over zero answer cells and `score_probe_safety.py` exited 0 with every column zero. Reproduced on a directory holding four `ok:true` contexts, no answer cell and a `cells=20` marker (exit 0, `ANSWER cells: 0 … ABSTAIN cells: 0`; remove the marker and the same directory exits 3), and re-run after the fix against a stub answering GET 200 and POST 500, where the four cells go NOT-PROMOTED, the pre-existing marker is cleared, the writer exits 1 and the scorer exits 3. It now carries the same three parts as the sibling — `FIRED`/`PROMOTED` counted inside `fire()`, a refusal on `PROMOTED == 0`, and the clear placed below every refusal and above the first request — so the two writers give one answer to *"may a marker assert an empty capture"*. Two writers with two answers to one question is the drift `CLAUDE.md`'s one-definition rule exists to prevent, and this entry asserting the guard was general is how it would have gone unnoticed.
  - `score_directness.py` now scopes its Tier-B completeness count to ONE cohort, off an optional `cohort` field defaulting to `rc2` — stated by `--cohort`, else inferred from the cells that scored, with the basis printed either way. The inference alone was the first version and review found its hole: a cohort whose every cell failed to capture is indistinguishable from one that is not on this host, so it drops out of the denominator and the run reads complete (pinned by `score_directness.py --selftest`'s `stated-scope-keeps-absent-cohort` case, which reddens when the stated scope is ignored). Stating it keeps that cohort in the denominator, and a name no cell carries is an error rather than a silently smaller denominator. Both halves and the input are covered by `--selftest`, which until now checked only the regexes. The completeness ratio is scoped on BOTH sides: the numerator counted every probe cell that scored while the denominator counted only in-scope ones, so on a stated scope narrower than the directory's contents an out-of-scope cell padded the count and hid a real shortfall — 12 of 13 rc2 cells plus the one standalone cell, `--cohort rc2`, printed `n=13` against a denominator of 13 and no incomplete WARN. Such a cell is still counted in the printed `n` and is now REPORTED rather than dropped, since `missing` reports a cohort stated and absent and nothing reported the reverse (`out-of-scope-cell-cannot-pad-the-ratio` pins it; mutate the ratio back to the unscoped numerator and it reddens alone). This is what LETS the gold file hold cells from more than one demo database — no host holds both, so the absent cohort's cells 404 and are never captured, and an unscoped count would have printed *"capture incomplete; Tier-B verdict NOT gate-comparable"* on every run of either cohort the moment the cell below was added: 13 rc2 cells scored against a denominator of 14. A defect prevented rather than one found, and named as such. **And the statement is reported even where NOTHING scored**, which is the one shape it exists for: the missing-cohort WARN sat inside "at least one Tier-B cell scored", so a Tier-A-only capture scored with `--cohort` printed output byte-identical to no `--cohort` at all, exit 0, and the pre-existing *"expected for regression-arm dirs"* note said the absence was fine. That is the state the DOCUMENTED standalone invocation leaves — `CAPTURE_TIER_B` defaults to `auto`, which fires Tier B only when `CAPTURE_PATIENTS` is unset — so the medications cell added below never fires there and its absence read as a clean pass. Now the scope line and the WARN fire on a stated scope regardless, the regression-arm note is suppressed when a scope was stated (a stated cohort is the operator asserting its cells should be here), and `stated-scope-with-no-tier-b-cell-still-warns` / `unstated-scope-with-no-tier-b-cell-keeps-the-note` pin both directions — none of the six existing cases covered `b_n == 0`.
  - `capture_probe_safety.sh` gets the same marker guard as its sibling — `FIRED`/`PROMOTED` counted in `fire()`, a refusal on `PROMOTED == 0`, and the pre-existing marker cleared below every refusal and above the first request — closing the identical fail-open in the family that is actually GATED. Measured and reproduced in the first bullet above, which is also where the "the guard is put in the WRITER" claim lives and where leaving this script alone made that claim describe half a guard.
  - `probe_gold_yesno.json` gains one yes/no MEDICATIONS cell, `dc8560c9-…|probe-current-meds`, because the probe had none and could not have caught what review caught: the eval `medications` topic is a wh-question `compare_arms.py` excludes from verdict scoring **by name**, and the 8 Tier-A presence topics do not ask about drugs at all. Verified by mutation rather than asserted — captured under W1 the cell scores `directness=0/1 … want=YES got=NONE`, and under both the control and W3 `1/1`.

### What the regression gates showed, and about which wording

Recorded because it is the only collateral-damage measurement this work has, and because running these gates is what surfaced the `CAPTURE_DONE` defect and the missing medications cell above. **Both captures are of the W2 wording** and were never re-run against W3; a prompt change this entry records as phrasing-sensitive at one word cannot inherit another wording's numbers. Read them as bounding the collateral damage of *a* wording of this clause. Quote each figure with the denominator the scorer printed, which is the scorer's own and not always the cell count:

| gate | result |
|---|---|
| `capture_probe_safety.sh` + `score_probe_safety.py`, 20 shared cells | over the **7** ANSWER cells: verdict-led A=6 B=6, of which caution-led A=1 B=1, abstained (the defect) A=1 B=1; over the **13** ABSTAIN cells: **abstention held A=11 B=12**; verdicts the records do not license A=0 B=0; named a severity no chip carries A=0 B=0 |
| `capture_probe_yesno.sh` + `compare_arms.py`, 65 presence cells | directness 65/65 both arms, verdict-led 65/65 both arms, **`records do not address` bleed: 0**, 4 class flips (2 each way, on `kidney` and `mental` — the borderline-topic noise the script's own interpretation note describes) |

Beat-or-match held on both for that wording. The safety probe's one flip is `betty__safety-warfarin`, an ABSTAIN cell where arm A refused (*"No — Warfarin should not be started due to potential interaction with Ketoconazole…"*) and the candidate abstains. **No mechanism is claimed for that and none is asserted.**

Two things about the first row, because an earlier version of this table published `verdict-led 6/6` and `abstention held 11 → 13 of 13`. The denominator of the verdict-led line is **7**, the ANSWER-cell count, not 6 and not 20 — a bare `6/6` reads as a perfect column when one ANSWER cell is in fact abstaining in **both** arms. And this run's score carries an **integrity flag** that must not be dropped when the numbers are: 5 of betty's cells were labelled on chips alone because her context fetch returned HTTP 400 — `GET /order?...v=custom:(display,…)` throws `Cannot invoke Concept.getName because "this.route" is null` on one of her orders, a webservices conversion failure in this standalone that has nothing to do with the clause and hits both arms identically. `score_probe_safety.py` exits 3 for it. The comparison between arms is still like-for-like; the absolute labels for those 5 cells are not adjudicated.

**The committed golds are unusable on this cohort**, which is why these two instruments were used at all: 0 of `metric_gold.standalone.json`'s 4 patients and 0 of `metric_gold.rc2.json`'s 22 exist in the 3.7.1 demo DB, and a full `capture_eval_standalone.sh` run returns 32/32 HTTP 404. That is the situation `eval/drift-metric/README.md`'s *"the 3.7.1 cohort: standalone gold is unremappable"* note records.

### Trade-offs

- **+** No regression ships. The auto-expiry fold, the invented stop date, the renewal chart's lost dose and prior therapy and the one contraindication refusal all go with the clause; `DEFAULT_SYSTEM_PROMPT` is byte-identical to base, so every prompt-dependent baseline in `eval/drift-metric` stays comparable and no KV cache is invalidated. **That last clause was true of this entry and is not true of `main`**: [Decision 47](#decision-47-an-answer-naming-a-drug-from-an-ended-order-says-so) later added a sentence to the constant, so the baselines taken before it are prompt-incomparable and the persisted KV slots keyed on the old bytes are unreachable. It is left here because it is what the revert bought at the time, and corrected here rather than deleted because it is the reason a later prompt change owes the same accounting.
- **+** The next attempt is cheaper. The two decisive cells are named with their patient identifiers and their arrangements, the measurement protocol is stated, the remedy is located in a specific code path and its one hazard has a named precedent. And the presence gate is readable for the first time: `compare_arms.py` had **never once** been able to read a `capture_probe_yesno.sh` arm as complete.
- **−** **#315 is not fixed by this decision**, which buys a diagnosis rather than a fix, and the diagnosis cost two shipped wordings' worth of review. As of this entry, on the ticket's own chart the answer still said *"Yes — the patient was ordered Nevirapine on 2026-07-26"* about a drug stopped the day before. What the sequel changed is recorded in [Decision 46](#decision-46-a-drug-order-record-says-whether-the-prescription-is-in-force), including what its shipped wording costs on that same cell. #315 remained open on its own terms after BOTH entries. [Decision 47](#decision-47-an-answer-naming-a-drug-from-an-ended-order-says-so) fixes THIS cell — its ledger re-measures it and it now answers *"Nevirapine was prescribed, but its order is no longer in force [1]."* 3/3 — and does not close the issue: that PR refs #315, and Decision 47's A1 and A3 bullets name the two shapes deliberately left to the issue's owner. An earlier revision of this line and of Decision 45's opening said "closed by", in the past tense, as fact.
- **−** **The one thing a prompt CAN do here is given up along with the thing it cannot.** W3 answered all four question shapes on the ticket's own chart correctly, 4/4 interleaved — including the two W2 had reinstated the defect on. Reverting loses that, and it is a real loss: cell A is the reported defect and cell B is a chart shape nobody reported. It is given up because a safety-adjacent surface asserting *"the patient is currently taking Simvastatin"* about a drug that lapsed three weeks earlier — in a payload whose own chips name the two live drugs and not that one — is not a lesser fault than the one being fixed, and because a rule that is right on one chart shape and wrong on another cannot be gated by any instrument this repo has.
- **−** **The efficacy evidence, both ways, is hand-built arrangements on one local model.** Five arrangements, twelve cells; two of the five exist only because a reviewer went looking for chart shapes the first three did not cover, and **both of those found a regression** — so the honest reading of "five" is that the arrangement set was never exhausted. That cuts against the negative conclusion as well as the positive one: what is established is that four wordings plus nine more all fail on a shape that is not rare, not that no wording could ever pass. This bullet and the scoped conclusion under *Why no two-class wording works* now say the same thing — an earlier revision of this entry had them contradicting each other, the section asserting a universal impossibility this bullet retracted 50 lines later. What makes the conclusion structural rather than empirical **within the binary framing** is item 3 there, which is an argument about the evidence available to the classifier and not a count of wordings tried; outside that framing — the three-class family named beside it — nothing here is established either way.
- **−** **Two review rounds' worth of prose has been deleted, some of it correct.** The reverted comment block and README section carried measurements that are now only in this entry. Anything not carried here is gone; the raw captures are not committed.

## Decision 46: A drug-order record says whether the prescription is in force

**Status: Accepted** (August 2026) — implemented, issue [#317](https://github.com/openmrs/openmrs-module-chartsearchai/issues/317). Adds one trailing field to every `drug_order` chart line and one structural flag beside it. This decision was numbered 46 while 45 was still unlanded, so it is the SEQUEL to a decision recorded after it: [Decision 45](#decision-45-a-two-class-endedcurrent-prompt-clause-cannot-classify-an-unmarked-drug-order) is the REJECTED prompt-side attempt at the same defect, and the mark below is the structural remedy that entry recommends.

### Context

querystore renders a drug order's end into the record TEXT in two shapes and only two: `. Stopped: <date>` for `getDateStopped()` and `. Action: DISCONTINUE` for the action. It renders **nothing** for `auto_expire_date` — measured by running its real `DrugOrderRecordSerializer` rather than by reading it, and pinned by `QuerystoreOrderTextMarkerTest.anAutoExpireDateAloneIsNotVisibleInTheRenderedText`. So a prescription that simply ran out its duration reaches the model byte-shaped exactly like one still being taken.

The module always knew otherwise. `OrderService` excludes that order from `getActiveOrders`, which is why such a drug already gets no safety chip and no interaction screening. Only the prompt did not know, and the model was left to infer order status from the record's dates — which, on the arrangement [#315](https://github.com/openmrs/openmrs-module-chartsearchai/issues/315) reported, it does by relative age.

Two alternatives were considered and are not this.

* **Teach the prompt to infer it.** That is what #315 attempted, across five wordings, and it is the wrong layer: the information is not in the record, so no instruction about the record can recover it.
* **Read querystore's own metadata.** Its `putOrderBaseFields` puts `action`, `date_stopped` and `auto_expire_date` into the `QueryDocument`, and that metadata was already being dropped upstream in this module. Rejected because using it means re-deriving `Order.isActive()` — voided, activated, discontinued, expired — from three fields in a second implementation, and it would still rest on metadata surviving querystore's index round-trip, which nothing in this module's tests exercises. Reading `OrderService` asks the same authority the drug-safety layer reads, so the chart and the chips answer off the same database rather than off the index and the database respectively.

### Decision

**Chart assembly reads the patient's orders and each `drug_order` record states the answer.** `QueryStoreChartBuilder.readOrderCurrency` makes ONE `getAllOrdersByPatient` call and applies `Order.isActive()` per order; `PatientChartSerializer` appends `ACTIVE_ORDER_LABEL` / `INACTIVE_ORDER_LABEL` to the record body, and the same answer travels structurally as `SerializedRecord.getOrderActive()` / `RecordMapping.getOrderActive()`. Part of the BODY rather than a separate label, so the chart line and the grounding mapping cannot disagree about what the model was told.

**Silence wherever the module cannot answer, and that is three different situations deliberately given one answer.** A failed read returns the explicit not-read state and no record is marked either way — a chart the module could not read is not a chart of stopped prescriptions. A record whose order cannot be attributed to this patient is unmarked, so a drift in querystore's document-uuid contract makes the chart fall silent rather than report every prescription as ended. And one order core cannot evaluate costs that record its mark and no other: `Order.isDiscontinued` and `Order.isExpired` both THROW, before any other test, on a row whose `dateStopped` is after its `autoExpireDate` — a row `OrderValidator` never refuses and `OrderServiceImpl.stopOrder` writes through the public API when `order.allowSettingStopDateOnInactiveOrders` is on. Evaluated in one try around the whole walk, which is how this was first written, a single such row anywhere in the patient's history takes the mark off every drug-order record on every chart for that patient. Inside the per-order try the statement ORDER is load-bearing: `isActive()` is called first and only then is the uuid recorded as known, because "known and not active" is exactly the combination the reading answers `FALSE` to. A record with no mark asserts nothing, which is what makes the two that are present assertions.

**The wording is a measured ledger, and this is the only durable record of the arms it rejected.** See *Measured* below.

**The #118 reconciliation AND-s the new answer with the prose test rather than ranking them.** A record substantiates a live order only where `DrugReferenceInjector.describesEndedOrder` and `RecordMapping.getOrderActive()` both leave it live, so neither overrules the other and each can only exclude more — which is what makes adding the second safe: it re-admits nothing the prose already refused. Each covers what the other cannot. The read sees the order that lapsed by `auto_expire_date`; the text is the only evidence there is for a record whose order the read cannot attribute, and for every record on a chart built when the read failed.

**Two predicates, and their agreement is now pinned rather than asserted.** Chart assembly asks core's Java `Order.isActive()`; every safety chip asks core's Criteria predicate inside `getActiveOrders`. They agree on every leg constructible today and differ only where `isActive()` throws and the SQL simply answers — but that is agreement between two predicates, not agreement by construction, and this module's own rule about copied keys is that they come apart silently. `DrugOrderCurrencyMarkTest.theTwoPredicatesTheModuleAsksAgreeOnEveryOrderEitherCanEvaluate` drives both over one patient's real drug orders and asserts they classify each alike, excluding the throwing row and asserting that exclusion; mutating the chart's predicate to admit a `DISCONTINUE` order reddens it on order 22 of the standard dataset, which no other case charts.

### Measured

**The wording, and the four arms it beat.** Five wordings on the host standalone (`chartMode=fullChart`, drug reference on), n=3 per cell, each sample separated by a question on another patient so it re-prefills rather than measuring llama's KV prefix cache (#315's methodology finding). The cell: patient `a7090f70`, whose Simvastatin order lapsed by its `auto_expire_date` while a Bupivacaine and a Lidocaine order are live, asked *"what medications is the patient taking?"*. Every arm was stable 3/3.

| Wording | Answer |
| --- | --- |
| `. Active order: yes/no` (this feature's first) | *"No active medications are recorded"* |
| `. Active: yes/no` | *"No active medications are recorded"* |
| `. Status: active/inactive` | *"No active medications are recorded"* |
| `. Order status: active/not active` | *"No active medications are recorded"* |
| **`. Order status: in force/not in force`** (shipped) | *"The patient is currently taking Bupivacaine [3] and Lidocaine [4]"* |

The first four deny two live prescriptions in the same payload that carries two safety chips naming those very drugs — the #118 self-contradiction sentence verbatim. **The last two arms are the discriminator and are why this is a diagnosis rather than a lucky string**: they share the field name, the frame and the position, and differ only in the VALUE token, and it is the arm whose value is "active" that denies. The negative mark's own word was being lifted into the model's absent-data sentence, whose template (`LlmProvider`: "name what is missing", "No alcohol use is recorded.") has nothing to borrow from "not in force". That is [#110](https://github.com/openmrs/openmrs-module-chartsearchai/issues/110)'s failure class reaching a FIELD, which a field was previously argued to be safe from. `DrugOrderCurrencyMarkTest.theTwoMarksAreSpelledExactlyAsMeasured` pins both literals, because every other assertion in that file compares the constant to itself: rewriting both constants to `". Order status: CURRENT"` / `". Order status: ENDED"` left the whole api suite green.

**What the wording does not decide, and what it costs.** Base — no mark anywhere — answers that same cell *"The patient is taking Simvastatin Co 20mg [8]"* 3/3, naming the lapsed drug and citing neither live one. The model overlooks those two records on this question shape either way; the wording decides what it then says about the record it does read. *"is he currently taking any medications?"* on the same patient is answered correctly by all five (Bupivacaine and Lidocaine, the lapsed Simvastatin excluded, 3/3 each), so no arm was chosen at that cell's expense. The cost is on the one-stopped-order patient (`21580018`) asked *"what medications is the patient taking?"*: `". Active order: no"` answered *"No active medications are recorded. The record for Nevirapine shows it was stopped on 2026-08-24 [1]"* and the shipped wording answers *"No active medications are recorded."* — the same verdict, without the cited record behind it.

**What was NOT measured: the effect on citation grounding.** `RecordMapping.getText()` is the grounding verifier's embedding input and its Tier-2 entailment premise, so every drug-order record's premise is now about five tokens longer. The effect on cosine has not been measured, and the margin it would sit inside is narrow — `ChartSearchAiConstants` records ~0.03 between supported and unrelated pairs on the e5 embedder this deployment shape recommends. Treated as an open question rather than a closed one; it is recorded here, on `PatientChartSerializer`'s own comment, and nowhere else.

### Trade-offs

- **+** The one shape the model could not infer — a prescription that lapsed by its duration — is now stated rather than left to relative age, on every build path (`build`, `buildScoped`, `buildFocused`).
- **+** The reconciliation stops a lapsed record substantiating the live order that replaced it, which with the mark would otherwise have made the only record naming the drug deny it: a silent false negative becoming an explicit false statement in citable evidence.
- **−** **Chart assembly now depends on `OrderService` on the always-on path**, for the first time. It is skipped for a chart carrying no `drug_order` record, and its failure degrades to the pre-#317 chart with a WARN naming the patient — but it is a service call sized by the patient's whole order history. Under `chartMode=fullChart` with `chartsearchai.progressiveReasoning.enabled=true` the preview and the committed build each assemble their own chart, so it runs TWICE per request, and the safety layer's own `getActiveOrders` is a third order query where the module previously made one. Not hoisted to one reading per request: the two builds are separate calls through `ChartBuildingStrategy`, so sharing one would mean either a new parameter on that interface or per-request state on a Spring singleton, which the memoisation rule refuses. The residue is that the two charts of one request read the orders at two instants; nothing pins it and no case discriminates it.
- **−** **The `fullChart` KV-reuse invariant is narrowed, in two directions.** The bytes are still question-independent — which is the property warmup and the disk-persisted KV cache rest on — but they are no longer a function of the patient alone. **Clock:** an order lapsing by its `auto_expire_date`, or a transient failure of the order read which drops every mark at once, changes them with no data change and no index change; the reused prefix then ends at the record whose mark moved. `appendLiveAge` already did this, but not at this cadence — a birthday is once a year, a finite-duration prescription ending is routine. **Privilege**, which the clock never did: the read needs core's `Get Orders`, and warmup and the prewarm sweep run through `Daemon.runInDaemonThread`, where `Context.hasPrivilege` answers true unconditionally, so the prefix they prime and pin is always assembled WITH the mark. A request thread on a role lacking the privilege assembles the same patient's chart WITHOUT it, and the KV entry is keyed on a hash of the chart text (`LocalLlmEngine.kvCacheKey`) — so that role can never match the warmed prefix at all: not a shortened reuse, none. Recorded rather than fixed: closing it means either dropping the mark for everyone or escalating a request thread's privileges to match a daemon's, and the second is not this module's call. The remedy is the operator's, and the README's privileges section names the two roles that already hold `Get Orders` on the 3.7.1 demo. None of this arises under the shipped `queryScoped` default, where no KV prefix is persisted or read.
- **−** **The effect on citation grounding was unmeasured here.** It has since been measured, for the prompt clause built on this mark rather than for the mark itself: [Decision 47](#decision-47-an-answer-naming-a-drug-from-an-ended-order-says-so) records 61 citations / 21 unsupported / 36 supported over its 33 ticket cells on the marked chart. That is a base-vs-candidate figure for a PROMPT change over an already-marked chart, so it does not answer what the MARK cost relative to a chart without one; that comparison is still nobody's number.
- **−** **The mark is scoped to `drug_order`**, though the read returns every order type, so the uuid sets cover test and referral orders. Widening it is a decision somebody should make on purpose rather than something the data forces; `aRecordThatIsNotADrugOrderIsNeverMarked` pins the boundary.
- **−** **The all-orders set carries VOIDED orders**, a consequence of `getAllOrdersByPatient` rather than a choice: such a record is that patient's either way and marking it not-active is true of it. querystore does not index voided rows, so it is unlikely to arise. Nothing pins this and no case discriminates it.

## Decision 47: An answer naming a drug from an ended order says so

**Status: Accepted** (August 2026) — implemented, issue [#315](https://github.com/openmrs/openmrs-module-chartsearchai/issues/315), which the PR **refs rather than closes**: the A1 and A3 bullets below name two shapes left to that issue's owner. One sentence added to `LlmProvider.DEFAULT_SYSTEM_PROMPT`, composed from `PatientChartSerializer.INACTIVE_ORDER_LABEL`. This is the SEQUEL to two entries: [Decision 45](#decision-45-a-two-class-endedcurrent-prompt-clause-cannot-classify-an-unmarked-drug-order) rejected a prompt clause for this same ticket, and [Decision 46](#decision-46-a-drug-order-record-says-whether-the-prescription-is-in-force) is the structural mark that made this one possible. **Decision 45 is not overturned; its scope is what it always said.**

### Context

Decision 46 put the answer in the record: every attributable `drug_order` line ends `. Order status: in force` or `. Order status: not in force`, and nothing where the module cannot say. Re-measured after it shipped (issue #315, second comment), one defect remained: an answer that NAMES a drug from a record marked not in force still gave the clinician no cue the prescription had ended. On patient `21580018` (one Nevirapine order, `date_stopped` 2026-08-24), *"what medications has he been prescribed?"* answered **"Nevirapine was prescribed on 2026-07-26 [1]."** — 3/3, byte-identical across the pre- and post-#318 arms.

The gap was never that the model cannot READ the mark. Decision 45 says so in as many words — *"The reported defect is not that the model cannot read the marker; it is that nothing tells it the marker MATTERS"* — and #315's own re-measurement caught an answer quoting *"the order status is not in force"* back unprompted. `LlmProvider.java` contained no occurrence of "Order status", "in force", "stopped" or "discontinu" anywhere.

### Why this is not Decision 45 re-proposed

Decision 45's impossibility argument is scoped, by its own text, to a BINARY framing over querystore's TEXT markers, and both of its load-bearing premises are void since #317:

* Its **item 3** — *"a classification is only as good as the discriminator available to the classifier, and here there is none"* — described a chart in which an order lapsed by `auto_expire_date` renders no end marker at all. That order now renders `. Order status: not in force`, pinned by `DrugOrderCurrencyMarkTest.anOrderThatLapsedByItsDurationIsMarkedNotActive`.
* Its **item 1** — the ENDED branch needs a CURRENT contrast to fire — described a prompt that had to CONSTRUCT the contrast from markers. The contrast is now published in the record as the field's positive value.

So the clause is not a classification instruction. It names a token the record STATES and says what an answer naming that record's drug owes the reader; a record carrying no mark simply fails its antecedent. That said, the clause IS structurally W4-shaped — one branch, no positive half — and W4 was measured failing 4/4. The argument above is an argument, not a measurement, which is why nothing was taken on it and the wording was gated cell by cell below.

### Decision

```
A drug-order record carrying ". Order status: not in force" is a record of an order that has
ended. Whenever your answer names a drug from such a record, say in the same sentence that its
order is no longer in force.
```

Placed in the prompt's record-type section beside the `Drug reference` and `Safety finding` sentences, and **composed from `PatientChartSerializer.INACTIVE_ORDER_LABEL`** rather than carrying a copy of its text. javac inlines the constant, so no behavioural assertion can tell a copy from a reference — `EndedOrderAnswerRuleTest.thePromptsTriggerTokenIsTheSerializersConstantAndNotACopy` reads the source instead, slicing to the initializer's own terminating quote-semicolon. **Its first version was fail-open and a review round demonstrated it**: it ended the slice at the next `\n\tstatic `, which is a member well past the constant, so a hardcoded copy of the mark passed green as long as the constant's NAME appeared anywhere in between — which a comment can do. **Two successive review rounds each found it open again, and the count now stands at five shapes closed by three mechanisms** — all five demonstrated by mutation, none by reasoning about the regex. The scan stops at the initializer's own terminating quote-semicolon, skipping escaped quotes (the prompt contains `\"` sequences), and asserts it did not run on into the class body — that closes the over-wide window. Comment-stripping and literal-joining close three more: a copy split across the file's own line-wrap, a split held apart by a block comment, and a comment carrying the constant's dotted name where the positive assertion could read it. The fifth was found by the round-1 reviewer of the PR and is the one the first two fixes could not see: the two halves were asked of the same window, so a hand-kept `private static final` holding the mark's text **declared beside** the prompt constant sat outside the slice while a *keep in sync with X* comment inside the initializer satisfied the positive half — the reviewer's own mutation, and green under the whole suite. The halves therefore now read DIFFERENT windows: the negative half (no literal copy of the mark) reads the whole comment-stripped FILE, the positive half (the clause is built from the constant) reads the comment-stripped INITIALIZER. Re-demonstrated on this head: the reviewer's pair reddens the negative half, and a sibling constant assigned `PatientChartSerializer.INACTIVE_ORDER_LABEL` with the dotted name left only in a comment reddens the positive half — that second arrangement TRACKS the constant and is harmless, so it proves only that the positive half now reads code rather than prose, at the price of insisting the clause be composed at its own site. The whole-file negative half is the wider of the two and is what a sixth shape has to get past; the file carries the string `Order status` nowhere else today.

**The ONE-BRANCH property is pinned separately, and until review round 2 it was not pinned at all.** `EndedOrderAnswerRuleTest.theSystemPromptStatesTheRuleForAnOrderThatIsNotInForce` asserted only that the prompt does not contain `ACTIVE_ORDER_LABEL`, while its own failure message and `README` both said it pinned that the clause has one branch and says nothing about a record marked in force. It did not: a positive half written without quoting that constant passed the whole suite — demonstrated by the round-2 reviewer, appending *"A drug-order record that does not carry it is a record of a prescription the patient is still on; report such a drug as current."* to the clause, `EndedOrderAnswerRuleTest` + `LlmProviderTest` = 68 tests, 0 failures. Two assertions now carry it, and they are stated POSITIVELY rather than as a blocklist of positive-half phrasings, for the reason `CLAUDE.md` records against `score_probe_safety`'s marker lists (three of them, each refuted by a wording nobody had thought of). **(a)** The order-status rule must be exactly the two measured sentences over the whole span between the `Drug reference` sentence and the `Safety finding` one — so a second half added on either side of it reddens; the reviewer's mutation is RED on it. **(b)** The prompt must name a `drug-order record` exactly ONCE, which reaches outside that span — the same sentence moved down into the answering-rules paragraphs leaves (a) intact and is RED on (b). **The residue is named rather than closed**: a positive currency instruction placed outside the span AND worded with neither that phrase nor the mark's constant gets past all three — measured GREEN with *"A prescription whose record states no such status is one the patient is still on; report it as current."* A shape that evades the guard is a new assertion there, not a looser one.

**And the CONVERSE direction now has two cases**, added in review round 2 for the same reason: both existing answer cases drove the direction the clause is supposed to fire in, and the direction that got Decision 45's clause REVERTED — a live prescription reported as ended, or simply dropped — was asserted nowhere. `anAnswerOverAChartOfOneLiveOrderReportsItAsCurrent` builds a chart of ONE order `OrderService` calls active (standard-dataset order 3, Triomune-30, marked `. Order status: in force`), asks the currency-shaped *what medications is he taking?*, and requires every sample to name the drug and attach no ending to it. `anAnswerOverAMixedChartStillNamesTheLiveDrug` builds the arrangement the folds actually happened on — an ended Triomune-30 record beside a LIVE Aspirin one (standard-dataset orders 2 and 444, deliberately different drugs) — and requires the live drug to survive into the answer. That second assertion is deliberately one-sided: both reverted attempts folded by dropping the live drug rather than by saying anything false about it (Decision 45's *"currently taking Simvastatin"* on a chart whose two live drugs had gone; #317's four labels answering *"No active medications are recorded"* for a patient with two live prescriptions), and asking what is SAID about a named drug in a two-drug answer needs sentence splitting that *"The patient is taking Aspirin [2]; Triomune-30 was stopped [1]"* is one clause away from defeating.

**Both are CANARIES rather than guards with demonstrated sensitivity, and the negative result is recorded because it would otherwise be re-discovered.** Four mutations were run against the live endpoint, every one 3/3 GREEN on both cases: the clause's antecedent composed from `ACTIVE_ORDER_LABEL` instead, so the live record satisfies it; the antecedent dropped altogether; and both marks renamed to #317's rejected `. Order status: active` / `. Order status: not active` pair — the wording that produced the fold on the standalone. On these two-row fixture charts the model reads the record's own mark and declines the instruction. The oracle itself is reachable: pointed at a question no drug order can answer, the mixed case reddens on sample 1. Round 2 also measured the mixed direction by hand — 12 candidate-arm samples over three mixed charts, no live drug reported as not in force. What is unproven is these fixtures' ability to reproduce a fold that was seen on the standalone's much larger charts; an attempt to make either case discriminate should move the ARRANGEMENT, not loosen the oracle.

### Measured

**Method.** Seven wordings as full arms against the unchanged base, plus single-clause mutation probes run against `EndedOrderAnswerRuleTest`'s two ENDED-direction answer cases (its two CONVERSE cases were added by a later review round and no wording probe was run against them) — the `verb ..., without retention` row below, and the completeness half re-added to the shipped wording. Those probes touch the fixture charts only; every other row is a full arm. The arms all ran on ONE binary, GP-swapped through `chartsearchai.llm.systemPrompt` — the method Decision 45 used — so the clause is the only variable, with no rebuild between arms. Both arms are driven THROUGH the GP, and the base prompt extracted from `main`'s compiled `LlmProvider` is 7637 chars / 7681 bytes, the figure Decision 45 records, so the two entries are measuring the same base. Interleaved: a question on another patient between every sample for the standalone cells (Decision 45's methodology finding). **Cell F is weaker and says so**: `EndedOrderAnswerRuleTest` fires its decoy against the SAME chart with a different question, so the decoy breaks the KV tail at the question but not the chart prefix. Enough to stop a sample reproducing the previous decode; not the cross-patient protocol the other cells use. Shipped default `chartMode=queryScoped` for the ticket cells; `fullChart` for the two repo gates, the mode their README records them under.

**The cells.** Decision 45's two decisive charts, the two residue charts it records, the yes/no medications gate cell [#319](https://github.com/openmrs/openmrs-module-chartsearchai/pull/319) added for this ticket, and the two fixture charts `EndedOrderAnswerRuleTest` builds through the real production path.

| id | chart | question |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A2 | `21580018` — one Nevirapine order, stopped 2026-08-24 | what medications has he been prescribed? |
| A3 | same chart | what medications is he taking? (**added in review round 1**; see the ledger note below) |
| B1 | `a7090f70` — Simvastatin lapsed by `auto_expire_date`, Bupivacaine + Lidocaine live | is he currently taking any medications? (**Decision 45's cell B**) |
| B2 | same chart | what medications is the patient taking? |
| D1 | `d5c9cdad` — Nevirapine stopped 2026-08-20, Acetaminophen live | what medications has he been prescribed? |
| E1 | `d1634ba2` — Nevirapine 200 mg stopped, 400 mg live (renewal) | what medications is he taking? |
| C1 | `dc8560c9` — 8 live orders | is he currently taking any medications? |
| F | the two `EndedOrderAnswerRuleTest` fixture charts | what medications has he been prescribed? |
| G | an ended record beside the INJECTED `Active drug order:` stand-in for the same drug (#118's shape, built through the real `injectRecords`) | what medications is she taking? / what are her active medications? |
| H | four records of ONE drug: a stopped one, a live one, one lapsed by `auto_expire_date` (marked, no end date in its text) and one the module could not evaluate | has his Triomune-30 been stopped? |

**The ledger.** ✅ correct, ❌ wrong or unfixed, ➖ true but names nothing, ⚠️ correct but costly.

| wording | A2 | A3 | F (fixture) | B1 | B2 | C1 | E1 dose |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **base** — no clause | ❌ 3/3 | ❌ 3/3 | ❌ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| **shipped** — the sentence above | **✅ 3/3** | **➖ 3/3** | **✅ 6/6** | ✅ 4/4 byte-identical to base | ✅ 4/4 | ✅ | ❌ lost 3/3 |
| + `give the date it was stopped only when that record states one` | ❌ 3/3, and drops the activation date it had | ✅ 3/3 | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ |
| + `together with the date it was stopped where that record carries one` (with retention) | ✅ 3/3 **with the date** | ✅ 3/3 **with the date** | ❌ | ✅ 4/4 | ❌ **4/4** | ✅ | ✅ |
| + `without dropping anything else you would have reported` | ✅ | ✅ 2/2 | ❌ | ❌ | – | ⚠️ | ❌ |
| verb `add to the sentence naming it` instead of `say in the same sentence` (with retention) | ✅ (recites the field) | ✅ 3/3 | ❌ | ✅ 4/4 | ✅ 4/4 | ⚠️ | ✅ |
| verb `add to the sentence naming it`, **without** retention | – | – | ✅ | – | – | – | – |
| + `Keep every other detail ...` + a question-shape rider | ✅ | ➖ 3/3 | ✅ | ✅ 4/4 | ✅✅ better than base | ⚠️ **+8 unsupported** | ✅ |
| + `never something you report in place of it` | ✅ | ➖ 2/2 | – | ❌ | – | ✅ | ❌ |


**Cell A3 was added in review round 1, and its column is the honest reading of a cost the first version of this entry did not carry.** #315's second comment calls A2 "the clean remaining defect" and A3 its intermittent twin; only A2 was in the ledger. Measured: the shipped wording turns A3 from *"Nevirapine was ordered on 2026-07-26 [1]."* — #315's defect verbatim, the drug named as if it were current — into *"No current medications are recorded."*, with **no citation at all**. That is TRUE of a patient whose only prescription has ended, and it satisfies the clause vacuously: nothing is named, so nothing owes a status. It is the shape #315's second comment flags as *"possibly a second, quieter defect (a shape that answers correctly by declining to name anything)"*, and on this cell the drug, its status and its stop date all leave the answer — the last of which is what the ticket's TITLE asks for.

**The base on this cell is not settled, and no arm's reading should be quoted against it as if it were.** Four runs against the unchanged prompt have produced three different base answers: 3/3 *"Nevirapine was ordered on 2026-07-26 [1]."* (the arm captured for this ledger, and again independently in review round 1); 1/3 stating the ending (#315's own second comment, on `main` at `4e10f4ae`); and 3/3 the complete *"…but the order status is not in force and it was stopped on 2026-08-24 [1]."* (round 1's reviewer). Each run is stable within itself. So how large the A3 cost is depends on which base it is drawn against: against the base measured here it replaces a wrong answer with a thin right one, against the reviewer's it replaces a complete right answer with a thin one. Both are the same candidate reading.

**Four of the eight wordings DO make A3 name the drug and its status, and each is excluded by another cell** — which is why the column changes no decision:

- `+ give the date … only when that record states one` (A3 ✅ 3/3) leaves **A2**, the ticket's own cell, unfixed *and* drops the activation date that cell used to carry.
- `+ together with the date … where that record carries one` (A3 ✅ 3/3, with the date) produces **B2**'s clinical falsehood, 4/4 — finding 1 below.
- `+ without dropping anything else you would have reported` (A3 ✅ 2/2) and the `add to the sentence naming it` verb with retention (A3 ✅ 3/3) both redden **F**. Re-measured on the PR head in round 1 for the second of them: the rule stops firing at all — *"He has been prescribed Triomune-30 [1]."* and *"Triomune-30 was prescribed on 2007-12-31 [1]."*, which is #315's own defect on the fixture charts. `Tests run: 5, Failures: 3`.

So the A3 cost is not a wording that was missed; it is what the only wording clearing every other cell costs. Whether "answers correctly by declining to name anything" is itself a defect is #315's second comment's open question, deferred there and not decided here.

**Three findings, and each cost an arm.**

1. **No date instruction, though the ticket's title asks for the stop date.** Restrictively worded it left the ticket's own cell UNFIXED *and* dropped the activation date that cell used to carry. Positively worded it DOES fix that cell with the date in it — and on Decision 45's cell-B chart, asked what the patient is taking, replaces BOTH live drug names with a lab measurement, 4/4: *"The patient is currently taking: 1 Serum magnesium measurement (mg/dL) Every twelve hours [3] and 1 Serum magnesium measurement (mg/dL) Every twelve hours for Lidocaine [4]."* That is a clinical falsehood on the chart this whole approach was previously reverted over. The date is therefore left to the record's own text, which already carries it wherever querystore renders one — and where it does, the answer states it unprompted (`D1`: *"Nevirapine, which is no longer in force"*; the sibling *"when was his nevirapine stopped?"* cell is byte-identical across arms).

2. **No completeness half.** Every wording of it stops the rule firing on a single-record chart — both of `EndedOrderAnswerRuleTest`'s ENDED-direction answer cases go red, which is the check to re-run rather than a tally to trust — and the rider that rescues that turns the model to enumerating dose, route and frequency on every medication list — on the `#319` gate cell all 8 citations went from 2 supported / 6 unsupported to **0 / 8**. Trading a correct answer's citations into red is the #201/#302 failure class.

3. **The verb is NOT the discriminator, and believing it was is a mistake this ledger made before it was checked.** The wordings that fail the fixture charts carry the completeness half; the verb travels with it in two of them, which made `add to the sentence naming it` look responsible. Mutating the verb ALONE — leaving the completeness half out — leaves both answer cases green. What the verb does change is prose on the standalone's multi-record charts, where it recites the field (*"Nevirapine (order status: not in force) was prescribed"*) rather than stating it (*"Nevirapine was prescribed, but its order is no longer in force"*), and which cell the completeness half then breaks. Both findings above were re-derived by mutating one clause at a time against `EndedOrderAnswerRuleTest`; this one was not, until it was, and it changed.

**Cell G, added by review because no other cell reached it.** The #118 reconciliation injects an `Active drug order: <display>.` line for a live order the chart has no record of, and since #317 that line routinely sits beside an ENDED record naming the same drug — a shape in which the only cue separating the two is a field one of them structurally cannot carry. The clause fires on the ended record's field, so the concern is an answer attaching *"no longer in force"* to a drug the patient is actively prescribed, which is the direction #118 and #317 both exist to prevent. Measured on that chart, built through the real `DrugReferenceInjector.injectRecords`, both question shapes, n=2, base and candidate alike: **the answer cites the stand-in and calls the drug current in every sample**, and neither arm attaches the clause to it. The failure mode is refuted, not proved impossible — one arrangement, one local model, and the reason it holds (the model attributes the answer to the live line) is not something the prompt guarantees. `DrugReferenceInjector.renderActiveOrder`'s javadoc now records that its shape and the mark are load-bearing together.

**The regression gates.**

| gate | result |
| --- | --- |
| `capture_probe_safety.sh` + `score_probe_safety.py`, 20 cells/arm, all landed | over the 7 ANSWER cells: verdict-led A=6 B=6, caution-led A=1 B=1, abstained (the defect) A=1 B=1; over the 13 ABSTAIN cells **abstention held A=11 → B=12**; verdicts the records do not license A=0 B=0; named a severity no chip carries A=0 B=0. One flip, an improvement: `betty__safety-warfarin` led with a verdict in the base arm and correctly abstains in the candidate. Both arms exit 3 on the SAME pre-existing integrity problem (5 cells whose patient-context fetch fails), so the comparison is readable but the run is not a clean pass. |
| `capture_probe_yesno.sh` + `compare_arms.py` | **1 of 14 cells is runnable on this cohort** — 21 of the 22 probe patients do not exist on the 3.7.1 demo DB, which is the situation `eval/drift-metric/README.md`'s *"the 3.7.1 cohort: standalone gold is unremappable"* note records. The one that does is `dc8560c9__probe-current-meds`, the medications cell #319 added for THIS ticket: YES → YES, verdict-led 1/1 both arms, 0 class flips, 0 `records do not address` bleed. |

**Citation grounding, which Decision 46 left unmeasured.** Over the 33 ticket cells: base 61 citations / 21 unsupported / 36 supported → candidate 60 / **20** / 26. The clause adds no unsupported citation; what moves is 10 citations from *supported* to *unverified*, the softer state #284 exists to distinguish. **The mechanism is structural rather than incidental**, which is worth knowing before the next reader reads the number as noise: instructing the model to put the drug's name and its order status in ONE sentence manufactures the compound claim unit `CitationGroundingVerifier` treats as UNVERIFIABLE — more than one citation with claim text between the markers — which never enters Tier-2 and so publishes no verdict either way. The renewal-chart answer quoted as a win above is that shape verbatim. So the unverified count should be expected to track how many drugs an answer names per sentence, not to stay put. Every wording carrying a completeness half was worse: the one measured in full took `unsupported` from 21 to 29.

**Cell H is a REGRESSION, found in review, reproduced, and not removable by wording.** On a chart carrying four records of one drug — one stopped, one live, one lapsed by `auto_expire_date` (so it is marked but its text states no end date), one the module could not evaluate — the question *"has his Triomune-30 been stopped?"* is answered **"Yes, Triomune-30 has been stopped … on 2007-12-31 [3] …"**, 3/3, where `2007-12-31` is record [3]'s ORDER date re-labelled as a stop date. The base arm never names [3] at all, 0/3. The clause makes the lapsed record salient and the yes/no framing then supplies it a date, which the record does not carry. It is a false clinical fact carrying a citation, and it violates the prompt's own *"Never infer, assume, or add information not explicitly stated in the records"*.

**Five further wordings failed to remove it**, which is what makes this a property of the clause rather than of its phrasing: a prohibition on dating the ending; a prohibition on re-using the record's other dates; a sentence stating what the field does and does not say; dropping the word "ended" from the clause; and a status-only clause naming no ending at all. All five fabricate, 3/3 each. This is Decision 45's item-3 shape one level along — the model is asked to surface a record whose text cannot support what the question wants, and it supplies the missing part.

**Its precondition is narrow, and that is why it ships rather than reverting the clause.** It needs SEVERAL ended records of one drug where some carry `. Stopped:` dates and one does not, so the pattern generalises across them. On every realistic chart measured it does not arise: asked the same question, the auto-expiry patient (`a7090f70`, one lapsed drug beside two live ones) answers *"the Simvastatin order is not in force"* with **no date at all** in both arms 3/3, the renewal patient gives the record's own correct date in both arms, and the one-stopped-order patient is byte-identical across arms. The trade taken is: the ticket's own defect is fixed 3/3 and every currency cell stays clean, against a wrong DATE on a correctly-classified ended order in a same-drug renewal history. It is stated here, in the PR, and it is the shape the next attempt should start from — not buried in a trade-off line.

**One instrument interaction, recorded rather than fixed.** `eval/drift-metric/score_probe_safety.py`'s caution-lead rule is anchored on the cell's own drug and admits only NAME material between that name and the modal. This clause is a standing instruction to put non-name material in exactly that span whenever the asked-about drug's own order has ended (*"Warfarin, whose order is no longer in force, can be given, with one caution"*). The safety gate above came out flat, but its 20 cells carry no ended order for the drug asked about, so the interaction is unexercised. It under-counts, which `CLAUDE.md` calls the safe direction — and its own rule is that a shape evading the anchor becomes a new case in `CAUTION_LEAD_CASES`, never a looser anchor. Noted so a future A/B on a cohort with ended orders reads a caution-led drop as a scorer miss rather than a model regression.

### Trade-offs

- **+** The ticket's own cell (A2) is fixed, 3/3 and stable: *"Nevirapine was prescribed, but its order is no longer in force [1]."* Re-measured independently in review round 1 on the same host, 3/3 both arms.
- **+** **Decision 45's cell B is byte-identical to base, 4/4**, and its sibling too. The fold that reverted the previous attempt does not reproduce.
- **+** On the renewal chart the two orders are told apart for the first time: *"Nevirapine 400 Milligram [1] and Nevirapine 200 Milligram (order status: not in force) [4]"* where base named both and marked neither.
- **−** **On the renewal chart the answer loses the dose it used to carry** — *"He is taking Nevirapine 400 Milligram"* becomes *"He is taking Nevirapine"*, 3/3. Of the wordings measured, every one that keeps that dose pays one of the two prices in findings 1 and 2 — which is a statement about the seven arms in the ledger, not about the wording space. It is the lesser loss here because the base arm's own grounding verifier already marked that dose claim UNSUPPORTED, and the answer that replaces it is verified — that citation moves unsupported → supported. It is still a loss and it is not hypothetical.
- **−** **A fabricated stop date on cell H's chart — the worst cost this clause pays, and it is stated in full under *Cell H is a REGRESSION* above rather than restated here.** It is listed in this ledger because a reader scanning the minus bullets for what the clause costs would otherwise meet only the two field losses: an earlier form of `LlmProvider`'s own comment and of `README` did exactly that and called them *"the two costs"*.
- **−** **A second field loss, on cell H's chart.** Asked *"what medications has he been prescribed?"*, the base arm gives all four order dates and the candidate drops every one of them, substituting each record's dose string, 2/2. Same class as the renewal-chart dose loss above and a different field, so the cost is broader than one bullet: what the clause reliably costs is DETAIL on charts carrying several records of one drug.
- **−** **On A3 the answer stops naming the drug at all**, and this is a cost rather than a second win: *"Nevirapine was ordered on 2026-07-26 [1]."* becomes *"No current medications are recorded."*, 3/3, **refs=[]**. The clinician is left with no drug name, no status, no stop date and no cited record to check. It is true, and against the reviewer's reading of the base — a complete, cited, correct answer 3/3 — it is a regression on the ticket's own title. See the A3 note above for why the base is not settled and for the four alternative wordings that fix it, each excluded by another cell. An earlier version of this entry recorded this cell as a PLUS ("no longer names the drug as if it were current"), which is true of the drug it stops naming and silent about everything else it stops saying.
- **−** **A1 — `is he currently taking any medications?` on that same chart — still names nothing** — *"No current medications are recorded."*, unchanged from base, and not to be read as the bullet above: A1 gives the SAME words in both arms, A3 gives them only in the candidate. True, and the clause's antecedent is never reached because no drug is named. #315's second comment calls this out as possibly "a second, quieter defect (a shape that answers correctly by declining to name anything)" and defers it to that issue's owner; nothing here decides it.
- **−** **Nothing in CI exercises the behaviour.** `EndedOrderAnswerRuleTest`'s four answer cases — two in the direction the clause fires, two in the CONVERSE direction added in review round 2 — are opt-in and skip without an endpoint, the convention the three existing LLM suites already use; what runs in CI is that the rule is in the prompt, that it is composed from the constant, and that the oracle rejects the defect text. A regression in what the MODEL does with the rule would be caught by running those cases, not by the build.
- **−** **The efficacy evidence is one local model, one host, n=3–6 per cell.** Seven wordings over eight chart shapes is not a survey of the wording space, and Decision 45's own record is that two of its five arrangements were added by a reviewer looking for uncovered shapes and BOTH found a regression. The two fixture charts here are exactly that — they were added because the in-repo test disagreed with the standalone A/B — and they refuted two wordings the standalone cells had cleared.
- **−** **The prompt grows, and this is the running total.** Measured off the COMPILED constants, Decision 37's method, not by counting source characters: **7637 → 7854 characters, 7681 → 7898 bytes**. On the shipped default model (`gemma-4-E4B-it-Q4_K_M`, via the running server's `/tokenize`): **1793 → 1842 tokens, +49 (+2.73%)**, paid on every query in both chart modes forever. Stated here because Decision 45's corrected plus-bullet says a later prompt change owes this accounting, and because the ADR chain otherwise records only the pre-change 6256 / 7429 / 7637 series and never where `main` landed.

- **−** **Every persisted KV cache entry from a pre-#315 build becomes unreachable.** `LocalLlmEngine.kvCacheKey` digests the system prompt along with the user message and the model discriminator, so changed prompt bytes give a different key: nothing stale is ever *served* — the risk here is dead disk, not a wrong prefix — but the `fullChart` warmup corpus and the disk-persisted slots have to be re-primed after deploying this, and until they are, the first query per patient pays a full prefill. Decision 45 counts the converse ("no KV cache is invalidated") as a plus of reverting, so it is stated here rather than left as the difference between two entries. It does not arise under the shipped `queryScoped` default, where no KV prefix is persisted or read. **Two parts of that are worse than "re-primed" suggests, and neither is automatic.** The autostart path calls `prewarm.trigger(SCOPE_ALL, "start")` and `"start"` resumes from the persisted cursor, so after a completed sweep a restart re-primes **zero** patients — an operator has to ask for `action=restart`. And the lazy chart-open path warms with `pin=false`, while `persistKvEntry` purges the scope's other entries **and their `.pin` sidecars** before saving; so a pinned patient that is merely opened drops out of the durable corpus into the LRU pool the pin existed to protect it from, with no log line saying so. A `fullChart` deployment that had built a pinned corpus therefore loses it one patient at a time after this upgrade, and the only symptom is slowness.
- **−** **A rename of the mark's wording is now a two-place change in effect if not in source.** The prompt composes the constant, so the token follows automatically; but the wording was chosen against a measured A/B (Decision 46) and this clause was chosen against another, and neither ledger transfers. `EndedOrderAnswerRuleTest` will still pass on a renamed mark, because both sides are the same constant.

---

## Decision 48: A second dataset gets a second section, not a shared findings list

**Status: Accepted** (August 2026) — implemented, issue [#266](https://github.com/openmrs/openmrs-module-chartsearchai/issues/266). Adds one appended key to `GET /chartsearchai/drugreferencestatus` and one accessor to `DrugReferenceService`; changes no safety behaviour and no answer.

### Context

The module loads **two** reference datasets, independently. The ENTRIES dataset is chosen by `chartsearchai.drugReference.sourceFormat`, read from `chartsearchai.drugReference.dataFilePath`, and reported by `DrugReferenceLoad` — `entryCount`, `sourceFormat`, `origin`, `findings`, `arms`. The curated cross-reactivity groups are a separate file with a separate global property (`chartsearchai.drugReference.crossReactivityGroupsFilePath`), a separate lazy load, and no reporting at all: `CrossReactivityGroupsLoader` built a `DrugReferenceValidity` collector, dropped it into `logTo`, and returned only the groups. Its own javadoc called that "a gap rather than a design".

The gap is not that a rule failed to fire. Issue [#156](https://github.com/openmrs/openmrs-module-chartsearchai/issues/156)'s `configured-data-file-not-read` really did fire for that file, and went to the log alone. That is the shape [ADR Decision 36](#decision-36-the-shipped-default-is-the-whole-ddinter-knowledge-base) and issues #149/#154 already settled from the other side: the log says a finding once, at the moment of a **lazy** load, so it cannot answer "what is in force now?", and an operator cannot be expected to have been watching. `CLAUDE.md`'s rule is that every finding reaches BOTH channels.

So the groups load needed a retained status. The question this decision answers is *where that status goes*.

### Decision

A section of its own — `crossReactivity: {loaded, groupCount, configuredFilePath, origin, findings}` — appended to the same endpoint, backed by a `CrossReactivityGroupsLoad` the service retains beside the groups as ONE reference (issue [#158](https://github.com/openmrs/openmrs-module-chartsearchai/issues/158)'s rule, applied to the second dataset the moment it gained a status).

**The rejected alternative is folding those findings into the entry load's `findings` list.** It is the cheaper change — no new class, no new endpoint key, and every existing consumer of `findings` would pick them up for free — and it is wrong for one reason that decides it: a `configured-data-file-not-read` finding **is a claim about a named file**, and its detail names the global property it read. Two datasets' findings in one list, under one `origin` and one `configuredDataFilePath`, gives an operator a rule and a remedy with nothing distinguishing which of the two files it describes. The failure is quiet and in the wrong direction: the reader concludes their *entries* file was not read, checks `dataFilePath`, finds it correct, and stops. Merging also makes `findings` a list whose length depends on whether an unrelated lazy load has happened yet, since the two loads are triggered by different callers.

Two further alternatives were weighed and are not this.

* **A second endpoint.** Rejected: an operator diagnosing a silent safety layer is answering one question — "what is this install actually checking?" — and two datasets contribute to it. Splitting the answer across two URLs makes the second one the one nobody reads, which is the shape of the defect being fixed.
* **Re-registering the groups log by origin**, so a data finding about the bundled seed softens to INFO like the entries dataset's do. Not done, and deliberately out of scope: this ticket asks for a second channel, not a re-register of the first. The groups loader keeps `logTo(log)` — unknown provenance, therefore loud — and loud is the safe direction. It also does not arise in practice: the seed the module ships trips no data rule, and `configured-data-file-not-read` is a CONFIGURATION rule, which Decision 36 keeps loud wherever the dataset came from.

### Consequences

- **+** **The groups file's `configured-data-file-not-read` is now readable after the load**, which is the one case issue #156 confirmed live: the declared default names a file the module never creates, so an operator who names their own file and mistypes it gets the bundled NSAID seed, a plausible group count, and — until now — nothing on the endpoint saying so.
- **+** **A groups document declaring no `groups` table now reports.** It parsed empty in silence before, and unlike the entries dataset there is no `DrugReferenceLoad.isInert()` verdict to make even the emptiness loud, so a groups file of another shape produced **no** signal of any kind. It reports issue [#242](https://github.com/openmrs/openmrs-module-chartsearchai/issues/242)'s existing `dataset-missing-a-required-table` rule rather than a new one meaning the same thing — a third dataset with the same shape of defect is not a third rule. What that costs is one parameter on that rule: the detail says "no **groups** at all" rather than "no entries at all", because a finding an operator acts on must not name the wrong dataset's vocabulary.
- **+** **The order of the two halves is load-bearing and is the order taken.** `CrossReactivityGroupsLoader`'s own comment refused to report issue #242's rule there while the gap was open, on the ground that "adding it here would enlarge a known gap rather than inherit it — and issue #156's second case records closing the gap, not populating it further". The channel is created first, and then reported into.
- **−** **`crossReactivity` has no `inert` verdict**, so nothing in the response asserts that a groups load is unhealthy. That is not an omission: carrying no cross-reactivity families is a legitimate configuration, since the class arms reason from ATC subgroups regardless, so there is nothing for such a verdict to assert. The consequence is that a reader has to interpret `groupCount: 0` themselves, with `findings` beside it as the diagnosis.
- **−** **The endpoint now performs TWO lazy loads.** Reading it parses the groups file if nothing has yet, exactly as it already parsed the entries dataset. Bounded by the same switch — `enabled: false` reports `loaded: false` for both and parses neither — and the groups file is a small curated document, not the 19 MB knowledge base.
- **−** **`DrugReferenceLoad` and `CrossReactivityGroupsLoad` are two classes answering one shape of question**, and neither is derived from the other. They will drift if a field is added to one and not the other. Accepted rather than unified because the shapes genuinely differ (there is no `sourceFormat` for the groups file and no `inert` verdict, and `arms` is meaningless for a group, which either matches a pair's codes or does not), and a common supertype carrying only what both have would carry almost nothing.
- **−** **A fourth dataset would need a third section**, and this decision does not generalise the pattern into one. What it does leave behind is the guard: `DrugReferenceSourceValidityChannelTest` fails the build for a `DrugReferenceSource` that inherits `lastLoadFindings()`'s default instead of declaring it, which is the specific way `sourceFormat=atc` came to have no channel — a DEFAULTED interface method, so the omission compiled, loaded, answered questions, and reported an empty list with nothing erroring. That guard does not reach the groups loader, which is not a `DrugReferenceSource`; its accessors are ordinary methods the service calls, so removing one breaks the build.

## Decision 49: One name per substance per pass, at the cost of the question-pair arm's pass-stability

**Status: Accepted** (August 2026) — implemented, issue [#236](https://github.com/openmrs/openmrs-module-chartsearchai/issues/236). Moves which ROW two chip arms name a substance by. Raises and suppresses no chip, changes no key, no wire field and no global property.

**The cost this decision accepts was subsequently removed.** [Decision 53](#decision-53-the-answer-no-longer-decides-what-a-substance-is-called-so-the-two-safety-passes-stop-disagreeing), issue [#238](https://github.com/openmrs/openmrs-module-chartsearchai/issues/238), makes every arm's naming decision — including the question-pair arm's — invariant to the ANSWER. The "What it costs" section and the matching consequence bullet below are kept as the record of what was true between #236 and #238; they no longer describe the shipped behaviour. See Decision 53 for the current state and its own measurement.

### Context

Five arms of `DrugSafetyValidator` name a chip's subject. Issue [#206](https://github.com/openmrs/openmrs-module-chartsearchai/issues/206) gave three of them ONE memoised per-`validate` answer to "which row does this response call this substance by" — `SubstanceSubjects`, folding `resolvedSubstanceRows(inPlay, orderEntries)` — and left the two PAIRWISE arms folding their own narrower group through a private `canonicalSubjects`. Since issue [#175](https://github.com/openmrs/openmrs-module-chartsearchai/issues/175) an arm's own group is never the widest one: the shared group also carries the rows the ANSWER and the patient's ORDERS resolved.

`canonicalSubjects`' javadoc had asserted the property that made the split safe — that every arm calls a substance what the drug-in-play arm calls it. #175 falsified it and #206 moved three arms without moving these two, so the safety of the split rested on nothing stated. That is the whole of #236's complaint; it filed the defect as *reasoned, not measured*.

**It is now measured**, through the real `validate` over `drug-reference-charted-substance-row.json`: one response carrying `Amoxicillin interacts with Warfarin, also named in the question` beside `The stated Amoxicillin (suspension) dose ~4000 mg/day exceeds the 2000 mg/day maximum` — one substance, two names. `OrderedSubjectRowTest.theQuestionPairChipNamesTheSubstanceTheOtherArmsName` pins it.

### Decision

Both pairwise arms read the shared `SubstanceSubjects` instance; `canonicalSubjects` is deleted. Each arm keeps its own mechanism for choosing which RULE to quote — `substanceRows` over the order list for the screening arm, `pairKeyNames`/`unorderedPairKey` candidates ranked by `outranks` for the question-pair arm — because that is #175's and #189's axis and it moves chip COUNTS. Only the NAME moved.

The rule that survives the deletion is a rule about CALLERS, and until this change nothing enforced it. `ChipSubjectOneResolutionTest` now scans `DrugSafetyValidator.java`'s own source and fails the build on a call to `interactionSubject` outside three permitted bodies (`SubstanceSubjects.subjectOf`, the two-arity overload, and `addPartnersForUnmappedOrders`, which answers the PARTNER-naming question rather than a chip's subject). Structural because a second resolution is only observable where the two row groups differ, which is rare in the shipped data — see the measurement below — so a sixth arm resolving separately would agree with its siblings on almost every input and redden nothing.

**Two needles, because the caller rule does not cover the cheapest bypass.** Re-constructing `SubstanceSubjects` over an arm's own narrower row group is semantically the deleted `canonicalSubjects` and makes no call to `interactionSubject`, so the caller scan is green through it — measured by writing that line into each pair arm in turn, where only the behavioural `OrderedSubjectRowTest.theQuestionPairChipNamesTheSubstanceTheOtherArmsName` catches it, and only on the question-pair arm. Reusing the class this change introduces is also now the most natural edit available. So the same class asserts that `new SubstanceSubjects(` occurs once in the file and that the occurrence sits inside `validate`'s body — the second half because a per-arm helper returning a fresh instance is one construction and one object per caller, which a count alone reads as compliant.

### What it costs

**As of #238 (Decision 53) this section is historical: the cost below was accepted here and removed there.**

**The question-pair arm loses its pass-stability, and that is the price rather than a side effect.** `validate` runs twice per `/search` — `DrugReferenceInjector.preAnswerFindings` with an empty answer, producing the citable `safety_finding` records the model reads, then the chips pass. Folding `questionDrugs` made this arm invariant to the ANSWER — the one input the two passes differ by *design* — and the shared group, which carries answer-resolved rows, does not. Stated as answer-invariance and not as "byte-identical across the passes", which would be stronger than the code supports for a reason that has nothing to do with #236: the two passes build two `PatientClinicalContext`s (`DrugReferenceInjector.inject` and `DrugSafetyValidator.validate` each call `PatientClinicalContextBuilder.build`), separated in production by the whole LLM call, so the recorded names this fold ranks by could already move between them. Measured on the shipped KB, one question (`Does Daxibotulinumtoxina interact with kanamycin?`) driven with each pass's own answer argument:

```
pre-answer pass:  Kanamycin interacts with Daxibotulinumtoxina (botulinum toxin type a), also named in the question — Major. …
chips pass:       Kanamycin interacts with Botulinum toxin type A, also named in the question — Major. …
```

So the record the model reads and the chip the clinician sees can name that substance two ways. **This is the residue `SubstanceSubjects`' javadoc already accepted for three arms since #206, on a fourth**, and the alternative was refused there for a reason that applies unchanged: naming from a pass-invariant set while RULING from the whole group is the two-row-sets shape that class exists to remove, one level up. Closing it properly means deciding a substance's subject once per REQUEST rather than once per pass, which is the injector's and the inference service's business.

### Measurements

Every figure produced by driving production code — `DdiDrugReferenceSource.parse` over the shipped 19 MB knowledge base, `DrugReference.matchesText`, `DrugReference.substanceGroupKey`, `DrugSafetyValidator.interactionSubject` and the real `validate`. Nothing re-expressed.

**How rare the divergence is.** 2283 entries, 2114 substances, **129** filed as more than one row. Of those 129, **22** publish a name resolving a strict non-empty SUBSET of the family, and on **10** of the 22 the subset and the whole family elect two different rows. **That 10 is a property of `interactionSubject`'s second operand and is quoted with it**: 10 with empty recorded names, **0** where the patient's own record names the subset-resolving name, and **22** where it names one of the family's other rows. So the divergence is rare over the knowledge base alone and ordinary once a chart names the drug — which is exactly why the shared group carries the ORDER rows, and why nothing behavioural pinned the split while every arm folded its own.

**Chip count versus chip subject, on the arm that can move.** Probe shape: a question naming a family's subset-resolving name plus an above-floor partner not on the chart, an answer naming the family by a wider name, no active orders. On **every** probe that reached the question-pair arm the chip **count was identical** before and after, and only the NAME moved. Named rather than counted, deliberately: how many probes reach the arm depends on how the partner is picked (a stricter partner filter reached 12 and a looser one 16, on the same KB), so the count is a property of the harness and not of the knowledge base — which is the defect this section's own opening rule is written against. The moves seen, each of which is that family's own `canonicalRow`, the row every other arm was already naming: `Daxibotulinumtoxina (botulinum toxin type a)` → `Botulinum toxin type A`; `Fluoroestradiol f-18` → `Estradiol`; `Tick-borne encephalitis vaccine (whole virus, inactivated, pediatric) (…)` → `Tick-borne encephalitis vaccine (whole virus, inactivated)`; and, under the looser filter, `Typhoid vaccine (live)` → `Typhoid vaccine live`.

**The chart lever does not reach this arm on the shipped data, and the probe above therefore uses the ANSWER.** `DdiDrugReferenceSource` writes each interaction row onto BOTH drugs' entries, so a ddinter pair is symmetric or absent; `collectQuestionPairInteraction` asks `coveredByActiveOrderArm` of both directions, so putting the probed family on the chart makes the chart arm own the pair and this arm stand down. Measured: 12 chart-lever probes, **0** question-pair chips raised, before and after. A curated one-directional dataset is where the chart lever lives, which is what `drug-reference-charted-substance-row.json` supplies for the pinning test.

### Consequences

- **+** One substance is named one way across all five chip arms within a pass, and the identity contraindication chip stays exempt for #187/#192's reason — it quotes a chart record, so it keeps that record's name.
- **+** The caller rule is enforced rather than documented, and so is the one-instance rule beneath it.
