Verified by mutation: reintroducing a direct fold in either pair arm reddens `ChipSubjectOneResolutionTest` with the offending line numbers; so does re-constructing `SubstanceSubjects` in either arm, and so does hoisting the one construction out of `validate` into a helper.
- **+** Cheaper, incidentally: one fewer `substanceRows` grouping pass per `validate` (the screening arm had been grouping the same list twice), and `interactionSubject` folds are now bounded by emitted pairs rather than by each arm's whole row set.
- **−** The question-pair arm's cross-pass residue above. **Closed by [Decision 53](#decision-53-the-answer-no-longer-decides-what-a-substance-is-called-so-the-two-safety-passes-stop-disagreeing) (issue #238):** the arm now reads its subject off a naming group the answer cannot widen, so this bullet no longer holds.
- **−** A chip can now name a row the question's own word did not spell. That is the settled reading of a chip and not a new class of claim — a chip's subject is a SUBSTANCE (#162), and the module already reports what the TEXT stated under a row the text did not spell (#245, `The stated Amoxicillin (suspension) dose …` for an answer saying `amoxicillin`). The cost is the one #235 already recorded for the contraindication arm: a rule authored on one row is reported under the substance's name, a wider claim than that row makes.
- **−** The structural guard reads ONE file, and that costs the two needles differently. `interactionSubject` is package-private and static, so a chip arm written in another class of `…chartsearchai.reference` would call it unseen. Deliberate: the five arms all live in `DrugSafetyValidator`, and the blessed cross-class caller (`DrugReferenceInjector.chartAnchoredSubject`, which raises no chip) shows the package boundary is not the rule's edge. The construction needle has no such residue while the class stays as declared — `SubstanceSubjects` is a `private static final` nested class, so no other file can name it — which is why the guard asserts those modifiers rather than the name alone, and widening them reddens. Neither needle says anything about an arm that resolves a subject some other way — by calling `DrugReference.canonicalRow` directly, say, or by writing a second lookup class of its own. And both read the source as TEXT, so an indirection leaving the text unchanged is outside them: measured in round 2 of this PR's review, a lambda declared inside `validate` that closes over a construction and is applied per arm passes both needles, and is caught only by the behavioural case — which covers the question-pair arm alone, so on the screening arm that residue is uncovered. The needle for the resolver's own name was widened in the same round to match every syntactic form of it after a method reference (`DrugSafetyValidator::interactionSubject`) was shown to walk past the paren-anchored version with the whole suite green.
- **−** `eval/drift-metric/score_probe_safety.py` filters a cell's chips by matching `DRUG_ALIASES` against the chip's `drug` field, so a subject renamed into a different alias family would drop the chip and mislabel the cell. Not reachable today, and the reason is not the one it first looks like: the arm's gate is `questionDrugs.size() < 2` over ROWS, so a cell naming one multi-row substance does run it — it emits nothing because `DdiDrugReferenceSource.isSelfPair` drops same-substance rules at parse and `pairKeyNames` collapses the two rows to one key. The screening gate is never met either, since every probe cell names a drug. Named here rather than guarded, because the scorer would have to be changed for a shape that does not exist.

## Decision 50: An unmapped order's codes are narrowed to the presentation the chart records

**Status: Accepted** (August 2026) — implemented, issue [#234](https://github.com/openmrs/openmrs-module-chartsearchai/issues/234). Amends [Decision 31](#decision-31-one-shared-class-choice-for-both-arms)'s first rejected alternative.

### Context

[#228](https://github.com/openmrs/openmrs-module-chartsearchai/issues/228) gave the duplicate-therapy class arm a second leg, so it reaches an active order the concept dictionary mapped to no ATC code by resolving the order's NAME against the reference dataset. That left the two legs comparing on two different authorities. A dictionary-**mapped** order is compared on the one code the dictionary published for its concept — a specific presentation. An **unmapped** one is compared on every code the dataset files its substance under, which covers every route that substance is marketed as. `DrugSafetyValidator.sharedClass` prefers a subgroup that is not locally applied ([Decision 31](#decision-31-one-shared-class-choice-for-both-arms), issue #161), so the systemic one always won.

The reported consequence: an unmapped `Hydrocortisone cream 1%` was named as a co-medication in an `H02AB` systemic-corticosteroid duplicate-therapy chip, while the same order mapped to `D07AA02` correctly says nothing. Mapping an order **more** correctly made the module quieter, and the chip an implementer saw was the wrong one of the two.

### The ticket's own proposed remedy is refuted by the data

It proposed threading the route so the name-resolution leg could "prefer a row whose administration route matches". Measured through `DdiDrugReferenceSource.parse`, `DrugReference.substanceGroupKey()` and `normalizedAtcCodes()` over the shipped 19 MB knowledge base: **all 129 of its multi-row substances publish an identical ATC code list across every one of their rows, and none differs.** Hydrocortisone's family is THREE rows — `Hydrocortisone`, `(ophthalmic)`, `(topical)` — and each publishes the same nine codes. (`Hydrocortisone butyrate` publishes them too and is filed as a separate substance, which is why it is named here rather than counted in: reading it as a hydrocortisone row is the identity conflation issue #209 exists to prevent.) That independently re-derives the invariant `DrugSafetyValidator.entryForAtcCode`'s javadoc already states from the other side, and it means choosing a row cannot change any classification. The narrowing has to be of the **codes**.

### Decision

Carry the presentation, and narrow the codes with it, at one site.

1. `PatientClinicalContextBuilder` reads `DrugOrder.getRoute()` and the ordered `Drug`'s `getDosageForm()`, each in its own `try`, and `PatientClinicalContext.ActiveDrugOrder.getAdministrationTerms()` carries them per order. **Both sources**, because neither alone covers the shapes that matter: the route is a `drug_order` column and so is the only leg a non-coded order has, while the dose form is a `drug` column and is the only leg that reaches the skin — measured 2026-08-28, the 3.7.1 reference dictionary's "Route of administration" set has 17 members and not one names a cutaneous route.

   **Every name the concept publishes, not the one `Concept.getName()` elects.** That method returns the locale-**preferred** name ahead of the fully specified one, and on that same 17-member set three of the nine site-naming routes are preferred under a spelling that is not the formal one this module's vocabulary was first written in: concept 874 `In both eyes` (fully specified `Bilateral eye administration`), 877 `In both ears` (`Bilateral ear administration`), and 872 — the only vaginal route there is — `Vaginally` (`Vaginal administration`). `DrugReference.containsWord` takes no trailing letters, so the plural and the adverb miss the singular terms, and the first implementation of this decision therefore left the ticket's own defect standing for the standard bilateral eye and ear routes. Reading them all is the root-cause reading, and the words those three elected names are built on are carried as terms as well (`eyes`, `ears`, `vaginally`) — a second, independent mechanism rather than a substitute for the first, so a dictionary publishing only one of the two spellings still narrows; on this dictionary either alone reaches those three routes. Neither makes the term list complete and it does not claim to be: the same dictionary elects `OU`, `OD` and `OS` for its three eye routes, which no vocabulary of site *words* carries and which reach a site only because another name of the same concept does. The all-names read also carries the route-of-**entry** half: the same dictionary prefers `Rectally` and `Per NG tube`, whose fully specified `Rectal administration` and `Nasogastric tube administration` are what `ROUTES_OF_ENTRY` is spelled for. The widening is scoped to a helper this path alone uses — the order-NAME path keeps `addConceptName`, because what counts as a name there decides which reference entries an order can reach at all, which this decision has no evidence about. It is not locale-scoped either: a name in another language contributes nothing to an English vocabulary, while scoping to the session locale would drop the spelling that carries the site wherever a dictionary files it elsewhere. `ActiveOrderAdministrationTermsTest.everySpellingTheRouteConceptPublishesIsCarried` pins the read, and it is the one case in that class whose concept has a preferred name differing from its fully specified one — measured, mutating `addAdministration` back to `addConceptName` reddens that case's assertion on the TERMS and nothing else in the class; its assertion on the CODES no longer discriminates, since with `eyes` a term the elected name selects the eye on its own. The terms themselves are pinned by `UnmappedOrderAdministrationSiteTest.aRouteSpelledAsAnInflectionOfItsTermStillSelectsItsSite`, whose three legs each redden alone when their own term is removed (`In both eyes` and `In both ears` fall back to the systemic `H02AB` chip this issue removes; `Vaginally` stops selecting `G01AF02` out of the per-site pool).
2. `DrugReference.codesForRecordedAdministration` and `DrugReference.narrowsAnyCode` are the only two things that read those terms against an ATC code list — one narrows, one answers whether narrowing is possible at all — and they are built from the same pair of primitives, so they cannot disagree about what a record can express. `DrugSafetyValidator.codesForThisSubstancesPresentations` is the single production caller of both, reached from `addPartnersForUnmappedOrders`.

### The site table is a partition, not a list

`ATC_GROUPS_BY_SITE` together with `LOCALLY_APPLIED_GROUPS_NAMING_NO_SITE` partitions `DrugReference.LOCALLY_APPLIED_ATC_GROUPS`, deliberately, so it cannot go stale relative to the list it partitions. The criterion is that list's own, read the other way round: a group is locally applied when its published name says **where** it is applied, so the table records **what** that name says.

Writing it freehand was tried first and was wrong: it mapped the eye to `S01` and the ear to `S02` and dropped `S03` "Ophthalmological and otological preparations" altogether, which the shipped knowledge base uses — neomycin's ear codes are `{S02AA07, S03AA01}` — so an order recorded at the ear would have had one of its own site's codes discarded by a rule whose whole purpose is to keep them. That is the failure mode `LOCALLY_APPLIED_ATC_GROUPS`'s javadoc already records for issue #161's hand-picked list, reproduced one level along. `S` is the only prefix this table expands, into ATC's own three level-2 children — because its published name, "Sensory organs", names TWO sites at once. Not because it is written at main-group granularity: `D` "Dermatologicals" is too, and is not expanded, because that name says one site.

Measured impact per site over the shipped knowledge base (substances filed there / of those, the ones the table narrows / the ones already filed only there): skin 139/106/33, eye 134/101/33, ear 20/20/0, nose 24/24/0, airway 24/19/5, vagina 22/20/2, throat 12/12/0.

### The question is asked of the SUBSTANCE, over every order that names it

A partner is keyed on `DrugReference.substanceGroupKey()`, so two orders of one substance are ONE co-medication ([Decision 31](#decision-31-one-shared-class-choice-for-both-arms)'s neighbour, issue #186) and `alreadyACoMedication` skips the second before its terms are ever read. Resolving the narrowing from the order that reached the partner first therefore let `OrderService`'s list order decide the classification for both. Measured through the real `validate` over `ddi-contra-route-variants.json`, a patient on an unmapped `Hydrocortisone cream 1%` recorded `Topical` **and** an unmapped `Hydrocortisone 20mg tablet` recorded `Oral administration`, asked about dexamethasone: cream first raised nothing, tablet first raised the `H02AB` chip. The patient is on systemic hydrocortisone in both.

`DrugSafetyValidator.codesForThisSubstancesPresentations` asks it of the substance instead, over every dictionary-unmapped order that names it. **One presentation this module cannot EXPRESS declines for the whole substance**, rather than the union of the sites the others name — and the arrangement that pins that is a second order recording a bare dose form or nothing at all, not one recording `Oral administration`, which `ROUTES_OF_ENTRY` refuses a step earlier: the orders are presentations of one drug the patient is on, so an order this module cannot place is a presentation it cannot rule out. Failing the other way would let a topical cream silence the tablet beside it. Where every such order is one the data can express, the narrowing is the union of their sites.

**"Cannot express" is a stronger test than "names no site", and the weaker one shipped between two passes of this change.** An order the module places at a site the dataset files no code for — a nasal hydrocortisone, where the shipped KB publishes no `R01` — passes the weaker test, and its decline then rests on `codesForRecordedAdministration`'s empty-set fallback, which is evaluated once over the UNION of every order's terms. A sibling order that does narrow rescues that union from emptiness and takes the nasal order's codes with it. Measured through the real `validate`: the systemic chip stood for the nasal order alone and vanished when a cutaneous cream was prescribed beside it. `DrugReference.narrowsAnyCode` is the test that closes it, built from the same two primitives the narrowing itself uses.

### A term must name ONE site

`cream`, `ointment` and `lotion` were in the first draft and were removed. A cream is made for the skin, the vagina or the anorectum alike, so reading one as "skin" asserts something the record did not say — and reading it as all three is **worse** rather than more cautious: hydrocortisone's `C05AA01` then survives, dexamethasone publishes `C05AA09`, and a chip that said `H02AB` says `C05AA` instead, which is a haemorrhoid preparation and no more true. A form word is admitted only where the form serves one site and no other, which is why `inhaler` is a term and those three are not.

**`topical` went the same way, and it is the more consequential removal.** It does not name one site, and the module's own quotation of ATC is the proof: `LOCALLY_APPLIED_ATC_GROUPS`'s javadoc cites `C05A` "Antihemorrhoidals for topical use" and `C05BA` "Heparins or heparinoids for topical use", so ATC uses the word for the anorectum as well as the skin. Read as the skin it **silences** a real claim, which is the direction this rule must not fail in: measured through the real `validate` over `ddi-contra-route-variants.json`, an unmapped `Hydrocortisone eye ointment` recorded with route `Topical` produced no chip, where the same order recorded `Bilateral eye administration` produced the true `S01BA` one. The cost is stated in the trade-offs below and it is not small — `Topical` is the commonest spelling of a locally-applied route, and an implementation that has only that one gets no narrowing at all.

**A third reading of the word was tried and fails the same way: `topical` asserts NOT SYSTEMIC** — keep every code `isLocallyAppliedAtcCode` admits, which needs no new machinery, since `codesAtSites` already gates every candidate on that predicate and a site set equal to `administrationSites()` IS that reading. It is the most natural reading of the word, and the two refutations above are stated against readings that name a site or a set of sites, so neither reaches it as written; measured, it fails the same way, so it is recorded here with its own measurement. Through the real `validate` over `ddi-contra-route-variants.json`, with `topical` added as a term of all ten sites: the ticket's own input — an unmapped `Hydrocortisone cream 1%` recorded `Topical`, asked about dexamethasone — loses the `H02AB` chip and gains `Dexamethasone is in the same ATC class (A01AC) as active order Hydrocortisone`, a stomatological class the cream is not, which is the union reading's failure one subgroup along. And it silences a real claim in the other direction: the shipped knowledge base files nitroglycerin under `{C01DA02, C05AE01}`, so through the real `codesForRecordedAdministration` a `Topical` record keeps `C05AE01` alone — a transdermal patch a chart spells that way loses its duplicate-therapy chip against another nitrate. `transdermal` is a route of entry and would refuse that record, but only where the chart spells it that way.

Mouth, gut and anorectal have groups but no terms. `Oral administration` and `Rectal administration` are the systemic routes in the reference dictionary **and** the way an `A01`/`A07`/`C05A` presentation is given, so the recorded term cannot separate the two readings.

### A route of ENTRY refuses the site walk, rather than merely failing to match

Absence is not enough where a systemic route's recorded name **contains** a site word: measured, `"transdermal skin patch"` matched the term `skin` and narrowed a systemic presentation to the skin, silencing a real chip. `ROUTES_OF_ENTRY` is consulted first and refuses outright — `transdermal`, `sublingual`, `buccal`, `subcutaneous`, `intradermal`, `intravenous`, `intramuscular`, `intraosseous`, `intrathecal`, `epidural`, `parenteral`, `oral`, `rectal`, `nasogastric`. Same criterion as the site table, applied to the other half of the vocabulary: a term names a site when it says where the drug **acts**, and a route of entry when it says how the drug **gets in**. Refusing narrows nothing, so a term wrongly listed there costs a correction and never a false silence. The residue is named rather than claimed away: this is word matching after a hyphen strip, so it reaches `sub-cutaneous` but not `sub cutaneous` spaced.

### Why not

- **Suppress the co-medication when the module cannot attribute the route.** That would silence every unmapped order of the 157 substances the shipped knowledge base files on both sides of `isLocallyAppliedAtcCode`, on no positive evidence, in the direction a safety net must not fail. Declining leaves today's answer instead, and this rule can only ever remove codes, never a partner.
- **Read the order's NAME for the site.** `UnmappedOrderClassPartnerTest.thePartnerIsNamedByTheRowTheORDERRecords` pins a chip for an order literally named `Hydrocortisone (topical)`, and a drug name carries a presentation word for many reasons. The narrowing is decided by what the chart RECORDS as the administration, and that case is untouched.
- **Apply it inside `sharedClass`.** That method compares two code sets and has four callers; the narrowing belongs where an order is turned into codes, not where codes are compared. Applying it there would use the same evidence twice and once out of reach of the order it came from — and would still leave the question-driven half of that arm choosing by another rule, which is what [Decision 31](#decision-31-one-shared-class-choice-for-both-arms) rejected route-matching for and still holds.

### Trade-offs

- **It is measurably inert on every corpus this repo can drive, and its reach is narrower than "records a route".** Measured 2026-08-28: all 46 active drug orders on the 3.7.1 demo instance record either `Oral administration` (32) or nothing (14), and none names a locally applied site. Re-measured after the widening above, which reads every name rather than the elected one: those 32 carry route concept 924, whose five published names are `PO`, `Oral`, `By mouth`, `Oral route of administration` and `Oral administration` — three of them carry the word `oral`, so `ROUTES_OF_ENTRY` refuses the record and the corpus stays inert. The ticket's own `Hydrocortisone cream 1%` is fixed where the chart records a route or form naming ONE site — `Cutaneous administration`, `Skin ointment` — and **not** where it records the commoner `Topical`, nor the form word `Cream`, for the reasons above. Both of those are real spellings and both are declined deliberately, in the direction that keeps today's answer rather than guessing a site. The dose-form leg has nothing to record there on that install either: a search of its non-voided concept names for `%utaneous%` and `%Topical%` returns diagnoses, the subcutaneous route (920) and the DRUG concept 4264 `Hydrocortisone (topical)`, and no cutaneous or skin-named dose form at all. **A verifier should therefore expect the ticket's chip to be unchanged on a stock 3.7.1 install**, and this change should not be read as removing #234's symptom from one; what is exercised there is that nothing moved. What ships is the mechanism and the data channel; it fires on an implementation whose dictionary names the site.
- **A systemically-absorbed local presentation loses a chip it raises today.** Ophthalmic timolol against an oral beta-blocker narrows to `S01ED01` and the shared `C07AA` disappears. The same order MAPPED already says nothing, so this is the symmetry the issue asks for — but which side of that symmetry is clinically right is not settled here, and nothing in this module measures it. It is recorded as an open question rather than as a consequence of symmetry.
- **`isLocallyAppliedAtcCode` gains a second caller and a stricter use.** `sharedClass` prefers among candidates; `codesAtSites` can drop one, so that a `D01B` systemic antifungal is not read as a skin presentation. The difference is the evidence: the second caller holds an independent statement about the presentation the patient was given. `CLAUDE.md`'s bullet is amended to say so rather than being reinterpreted at the call site.
- **Losing the class sentence unfolds a chip.** Where the issue #88 fold had joined a rated rule and a class sentence about one partner, narrowing the class sentence away leaves the rule chip unfolded, and an unfolded rule chip is labelled by the rule's own match token rather than by the name the two arms reconciled (issue #292). One order, one name per chip either way; what moves is which rule decides it.

## Decision 51: The injected record names a folded chip's partner in its own vocabulary

**Status: Accepted** (August 2026) — implemented, issue [#297](https://github.com/openmrs/openmrs-module-chartsearchai/issues/297). Closes the deviation Decision 39 recorded and deferred.

### Context

`CLAUDE.md` states that `DrugSafetyValidator.partnerLabel` exists so that the chip and the injected record cannot name one partner two ways. Decision 39 broke that for a FOLDED chip: where `foldedPartnerLabel` reconciles the two arms it may name the active order by the class arm's ladder, and the chip reaches the prompt verbatim as a citable `safety_finding` (`DrugReferenceInjector.renderFinding`), while `orderedInteractionNotes` kept `partnerLabel`. Reproduced through the real `injectRecords`, DDInter excerpt, patient on `Aspirin 81mg`/`N02BA01`, *"Can I give ibuprofen?"* — the prompt carried both of:

```
{safety_finding} Safety finding — Ibuprofen: Ibuprofen interacts with active order
    Acetylsalicylic acid (aspirin) — Major. …
{drug_reference} Drug reference — Ibuprofen … Interactions: aspirin (Major. …); lisinopril (Moderate).
```

The two surfaces **cannot share one string**: the chip's name may be `DrugReference.displayLabel()`, and that label may not enter this record's prose (`DrugSafetyChipLabelTest.displayLabelNeverLeaksIntoTheRenderedRecordText`, `ReferenceRecordSubstanceCeilingsTest`). So the most that is available is naming one SUBSTANCE in each surface's own vocabulary, which is what the ticket asks for.

### Decision

**One fold decision, rendered in two vocabularies, carried to the record on the chip that decided it.**

- `foldedPartnerLabel` returns a `ReconciledPartner` — the chip's name and the record's — instead of a bare string. Its gate is untouched; only the rendering is doubled. A second copy of those conditions is how the chip's own two sentences came apart in #292, one level down.
- **Only the ENTRY rung moves the record's name**, to `OrderPartner.labelEntry.getName()` — coalesced with the rule's own token, for the reason the *entry rung's name is coalesced* bullet below gives. Outcomes 1 (`!namesADrug`) and 2 (the ORDER rung) hand it the rule's own token, which is what it already printed. One rule rather than an exception: the record names a partner by the dataset's name for it only where the fold has PROVED that name is this rule's (`unambiguouslyNames`), which the order rung deliberately never asks — `nameByOrder` does not update `labelEntry`, so on a renamed partner it identifies one drug while the label names another. Not "there is no dataset name to take": there can be one, since that rung is also reached after `soleSubstanceOf` resolved an entry for the order (`OneNameAcrossChipAndInjectedRecordTest.anOrderRungFoldStillLeavesTheNoteOnTheRulesOwnToken` reaches it with `labelEntry` = the real `Naproxen` entry; print it from the branch and read the marker). What `namesNamingOrder` HAS proved is that the token names that display, so the record's name is a *word of* the chip's rather than a second name — and handing the record a prescription DISPLAY would put a strength and a formulation the knowledge base knows nothing about into a list of that knowledge base's own partners, in a text that is quotable by construction.
- **The entry rung's name is coalesced with the rule's own token**, and that is not defensive padding. It guards a hazard THIS decision introduces rather than stating the module's position on a blank entry name: before it the note printed `partnerLabel`, which cannot be blank, so no blank could reach the note at all. The position itself belongs at the loader, where `DrugReferenceValidity` already rules on the neighbouring shape (`BLANK_ALIAS`, #150), and a rule there would additionally reach the chip's own label on such a row — which this coalesce cannot, as the last sentences of this bullet record. `partnerLabel` trims a `firstNonBlank` of two fields and so can never be blank; `getName()` has no such guard on this path, because `DdiDrugReferenceSource` refuses a row whose name `isEmpty()` and admits one that is whitespace, and `setName` does not trim. A blank there costs the note its partner's name in one of two ways, and which one depends on whether the rule carries mechanism prose — measured by mutation on both shapes. WITH a note the assembled piece is still non-blank, so `orderedInteractionNotes`' own `isBlank(rendered)` guard does not fire and the record renders `Interactions: (Major. …)`, naming no partner at all. WITHOUT one the piece IS the blank label, that guard fires, and the partner leaves the record entirely — the worse of the two, and what the COALESCE exists to prevent. Not something `orderedInteractionNotes` guards against: its `isBlank` drop is deliberate for a rule with nothing to say, and here that drop IS the mechanism by which the partner vanishes. No shipped parser produces both together today (`ddinter` synthesises a note for every row, `json` refuses a blank name, `atc` emits no interactions), which is why the fixture reaches only the first. What it does NOT do is make the two surfaces agree on such a row: the CHIP's name there is that entry's `displayLabel()`, which is blank too wherever the row also publishes no diverging generic, and a chip naming an active order by whitespace is issue #155's territory rather than this decision's — unchanged either way. Found in review, not by the suite; operator-data only (no row of the shipped knowledge base publishes a blank name, though one publishes a trailing-space one, which the note trims and the chip does not) and pinned by `OneNameAcrossChipAndInjectedRecordTest.aPartnerRowPublishingABlankNameStillNamesItselfInTheNote` over a DDInter-shaped fixture, since a hand-authored file reaches it at once.
- **The name travels on the `SafetyWarning`** (`SafetyWarning.reconciledPartnerNoteName`, set through the package-private `SafetyWarning.interaction` factory), and `DrugReferenceInjector.reconciledPartnerNoteName` reads it off the `preAnswerFindings` list `injectRecords` already holds. It is **not** re-derived. `injectRecords` runs the whole fold once through that call, so asking `DrugSafetyValidator` to walk `classRelationships` a second time in order to compare the two answers is the two-resolutions-that-agree shape #151 forbids — whose failure was silent and one-directional. Reading it off the findings also scopes the change for free: a name can only come from a chip that was actually raised AND injected in this same response, so an ORDER-DRIVEN record, which no interaction chip stands behind, is untouched rather than renamed after a chip that does not exist.

**Two conditions gate the record's adoption.**

- **The rule identity.** A note may take the name only if it is about the very rule the fold was decided on. `onePerPartner` collapses to one note per partner over ONE row while `bestRulePerPartner` chose across every row of the substance, so the two can elect different rules for one partner; answering null there leaves the note exactly where it was, which is what makes this change able only to REMOVE a divergence and never to create one. The accessor asks this of itself rather than trusting the caller, for the reason Decision 40 gives of `OrderPartner.recordNameSource`. **It does two jobs and only the second is a fail-safe.** Its first is to scope the name to its own note at all: `DrugReferenceInjector.reconciledPartnerNoteName` is a linear scan that asks every finding, so weakened to `rule != null` the FIRST reconciled name answers for every note in the record — the reproducer above then reads `Interactions: Acetylsalicylic acid (Major. …); Acetylsalicylic acid (Moderate).`, lisinopril's Moderate interaction under aspirin's name, in citable prompt text with nothing thrown. `OneNameAcrossChipAndInjectedRecordTest.aReconciledNameReachesOnlyTheNoteItWasDecidedFor` pins that, and was the one api case to redden on the weakening when it was made; mutate the conjunct and read the failures rather than trusting that. Its second job — the sibling-row election, where the record's row and the chip's differ — is what **nothing pins**: no fixture reaches the shape, because `ddinter` writes every rule's token and ATC from one partner row, so a hand-authored `json` dataset is the only thing that gets there.
- **Per-order structure on the context.** On the flattened shape of #118 the class arm's own reach is key-dependent: `orderPartners` reads the flattened set for its CODE rung and the per-order list for its NAME rung, so one prescription folds when a dictionary published its ATC code and does not when it published only its name. Two existing tests together say the chip may be key-dependent there and the injected records may not — `DuplicateInteractionChipTest.aRuleOnlyPairIsWordedExactlyAsBefore` ("the same aspirin order with no ATC mapping, so the class arm has nothing to say") and `OrderDrivenInjectionResolutionTest.oneOrderInjectsOneRecordSetWhicheverWayItResolves` ("the prompt behind those chips cannot depend on which key the dictionary carried"). The gate keeps the record out of it. It costs production nothing: `PatientClinicalContextBuilder` attaches the orders for every patient whose orders it can read — its null-patient early return builds a context without them, and that context carries no drug names or codes either, so it folds nothing — and #228 already made that shape key-symmetric.

**Closing the asymmetry itself was implemented and abandoned inside this change**, which is worth recording because it is the obvious next move. A NAME rung over the flattened set makes the class arm speak where `aRuleOnlyPairIsWordedExactlyAsBefore` says it must not; sourcing that rung from `findForActiveOrders` instead — ATC ∪ name, the list `CLAUDE.md` names as the one every consumer takes — additionally reddens `DuplicateTherapySelfChipTest.anOrderKnownOnlyByACodeTheRowItselfPublishesIsStillSkipped` and `DuplicateInteractionChipTest.aRuleOnlyPairIsWordedExactlyAsBefore`, because it re-adds under DATASET codes every partner the code walk already owns, which scopes the restating-existing-therapy skip's exact-code leg out of them (`OrderPartner.codesFromDataset`) and folds class sentences onto rule chips that had none. Whether the class arm should reach a co-medication by name on a context with no per-order structure is a decision on its own evidence, and it is not taken here.

### How far it reaches

Measured through the real `DdiDrugReferenceSource.load` of the shipped 19 MB knowledge base, `DrugReferenceService.findImpliedByDrugName` and `DrugReference.canonicalRow`: it carries 2283 entries and 590,312 expanded interaction rows over **2093 distinct rule tokens**; all 2093 name an entry, and **365** differ from the `getName()` of the row `canonicalRow` elects for the substance they name. So 365 is the CEILING on the tokens whose note text this change can move at all — the fold must also reconcile, which needs a class-arm hit and `unambiguouslyNames`, a strictly smaller population. The other 1728 rename to the same string case-insensitively.

Measured through the real `injectRecords` over the 16-drug DDInter excerpt, for every (subject, partner) pair: **240** injected records render an interaction list and **2** of them name the partner by the dataset's own name — both directions of the aspirin/ibuprofen pair, which is the only pair that fixture has tripping both arms.

Re-derive both by driving those methods; do not carry the numbers forward across a knowledge-base refresh.

### Trade-offs

- **+** One prescription is named one way across the whole prompt wherever a folded chip reconciled it: `Acetylsalicylic acid (aspirin)` in the chip and its `safety_finding`, `Acetylsalicylic acid` in the `drug_reference` note.
- **+** The prompt's name union for a partner still cannot GROW, and this is measured rather than argued. On the entry rung `OrderPartner.label` **is** `labelEntry.displayLabel()`, which is `name` or `name (genericName)`, so the note's new name is a substring of the chip's; and every element of `findings` is rendered into the chart unconditionally, with no cap and no truncation in `renderFinding`. Over the 600-cell sweep above, **340 notes were renamed and the new name appears in a chip of the same cell in every one of them — 0 misses**; the net prompt delta over that sweep is **−2790 characters** (a second sweep aimed at shortening renames gave +296 — see the bullet below on why the sign is the sample's and not the change's). What is lost is the rule's own token where nothing else in that prompt carried it.
- **−** **The note's name is longer than the token it replaces, and that costs mechanism prose out of the prompt.** `render`'s interaction section is a soft budget in which length decides which promoted partner keeps its full note and which falls back to the compact `name (Severity)` form. Measured by driving the real `injectRecords` over the shipped 19 MB knowledge base with the real validator behind it — 600 cells, 10 question drugs × 1–30 active orders × ATC-mapped/unmapped, dumped byte for byte on both sides of the change: **14 of the 600 cells flip a note from full to compact, 4298 characters of mechanism prose in total, and 0 flipped the other way in that sample.** Not one-directional, though — an earlier draft of this bullet said "strictly", and it is a property of those cells rather than of the change: of the 365 renaming tokens **142 SHORTEN** the note name, 218 lengthen it and 5 are the same length (`digoxin antibodies fab fragments` → `Digoxin`, −25; `acellular pertussis vaccine, inactivated` → `Pertussis vaccine`, −23), and `render` fits greedily on `used += piece.length() + 2`, which is symmetric in the delta — a shortening rename early in the promoted segment frees budget for a later note exactly as a lengthening one consumes it. A second 400-cell sweep aimed deliberately at shortening renames found 47 differing cells, 0 flips either way, and a net of **+296** characters, the opposite sign to the first sweep's −2790. So flips are rare in both directions and the net sign is a property of the sample; re-measure both directions after a knowledge-base refresh rather than only the one this bullet happened to observe. The reproducer is *"Can I give naproxen?"* on 13–19 of the sampled drugs, where `aspirin` → `Acetylsalicylic acid` (+13) at promoted index 4 pushes past `MAX_INTERACTION_RENDER_CHARS` at index 6 and digoxin's 294-character Moderate NSAID paragraph becomes `digoxin (Moderate)`. The shift is not bounded by 13: the longest entry name in that knowledge base is **110 characters**, so a note can move by ~100, and it compounds across promoted partners. Nor is the loss ordered by severity — the fit is per-note greedy rather than a prefix, so only position 0 is guaranteed its prose. **What is NOT at risk is `withheldInteractions`**, and an earlier draft of this bullet said it was: `bestRulePerPartner` filters on exactly the conjunction `promotable` applies, so a reconciled rule is always in the promoted segment, and `render` shows every promoted note plus at most one tail representative with no budget test on it — `withheld` cannot depend on a name. Measured unchanged in all 600 cells, and provable from those two lines. Nothing in the suite can see any of this, because every note-text case runs on a validator-less injector.
- **−** A partner whose winning rule the chip took from a SIBLING row keeps the rule's own token in the note while the chip names it by the ladder, so #297's own symptom survives there. It is the residue `DrugReferenceInjector.collect` already measures from the other side (80 of 121 multi-row substances), and the fallback is what keeps the change non-regressive.
- **−** On a context with no per-order structure the note keeps the token however the chip came out, so a folded chip there still names its partner two ways. Pinned as such by `OneNameAcrossChipAndInjectedRecordTest.aContextWithNoPerOrderStructureKeepsTheRulesOwnTokenWhicheverKeyItCarries`, so a change that closes it reddens a test rather than leaving this bullet the only record.
- **−** `SafetyWarning` now carries a `DrugReference.Interaction`. The wire shape is unchanged — `ChartSearchAiRestController.serializeSafetyWarnings` writes three named keys and nothing reflective — and the field is package-private with a package-private accessor, matching `carriesUnratedRelationship()` and `restsOnAnUncorroboratedChartMatch()`: a caller may set only what it may read back.

## Decision 52: A folded chip reconciles a token two substances name when one of them names it outright

**Status: Accepted** (August 2026) — implemented, issue [#296](https://github.com/openmrs/openmrs-module-chartsearchai/issues/296). Amends [Decision 39](#decision-39-a-folded-chip-names-one-active-order-once)'s over-cautious-refusal trade-off.

### Context

Decision 39 lets the class arm's label displace a rule's own match token only where `DrugSafetyValidator.unambiguouslyNames` holds: the token must name the ladder's entry **and name no other substance**. The second half exists for a measured reason — `DrugReference.isNamed` reads an entry's aliases, the `ddinter` parser writes those from the row's name AND its `rxnorm_name`, and the shipped KB's row named `Omeprazole` carries `rxnorm_name: esomeprazole`, so an identity-only test licenses printing one substance's rated mechanism under another's name.

But that second half is an EXISTENCE test, and on this knowledge base "the token names something else too" is the ordinary state rather than a signal. It cannot separate the two arrangements that matter, and they are opposites: the token is the ladder's substance's own display name and merely the rival's alias, where displacing is a spelling normalisation of one substance's name; or the token is the rival's display name and merely the ladder's alias, where displacing is the mis-attribution the guard exists to refuse. Refusing both leaves #292's own symptom standing on the default dataset — a patient on ketoconazole asked about osilodrostat reads `interacts with active order ketoconazole` beside `is in the same ATC class (H02CA) as active order Ketoconazole`, refused only because `ketoconazole` is also an alias of the separate `Levoketoconazole` row.

### Decision

Ask the RANKING instead. `unambiguouslyNames` keeps its `isNamed` gate and then asks `DrugReferenceService.uniqueStrongestClaimant` — the repo's one definition of "this row claims the name and strictly outranks every rival", previously private to `findNamedSubstances` and now shared — over every row of a DIFFERENT substance the token names. `DrugReference.nameMatchStrength` is what ranks them. A tie still refuses.

Two choices inside it, and a case apiece because neither can see the other's — `FoldedChipOnePartnerNameTest.aRuleTokenTheLaddersRowOnlyTiesKeepsItsOwnToken` reddens if the first is reversed, `aSiblingRowOfTheLaddersOwnSubstanceDoesNotContestTheToken` if the second is:

- **The ladder ROW's own claim, never its substance's strongest.** The label about to be printed is that row's display, and the rungs differ on which row arrives: `entryForAtcCode` hands over `canonicalRow`'s pick, while `addPartnersForUnmappedOrders` hands over the row the chart claims most strongly — deliberately the route-qualified one. Over the shipped KB, `Atropine` claims `atropine` outright while its `(ophthalmic)` presentation and the separate `Hyoscyamine` substance claim it only as an alias, so reading the substance's strongest row would let `Atropine`'s claim carry a displacement onto the label `Atropine (ophthalmic)` for a rule that may be `Hyoscyamine`'s.
- **Rows of the ladder's own substance are excluded from the contest rather than contesting it.** Otherwise an uncontested token starts refusing wherever a sibling row claims it at least as strongly — which on DDInter is every token that is not some row's own display name, the rows of a substance sharing one alias list.

### Two narrowings the ticket ruled out, and why this is neither

- **Scope the test to ATC codes.** `Levoketoconazole` publishes the same four codes as `Ketoconazole`, so a code-scoped test refuses this pair too.
- **Ask whether the rival substance is actually prescribed.** Tried during #295's review: it fixes the ketoconazole case and reintroduces the `Omeprazole`/`Esomeprazole` `A02BC05` displacement, reddening `aRuleWhoseTokenNamesTwoSubstancesKeepsItsOwnToken`. The ranking never consults the patient's orders at all — the comparison is between reference rows only — which is why it clears that objection rather than answering it.

### Measured

Through the real `DdiDrugReferenceSource` parse of the shipped 19 MB knowledge base and the real `DrugSafetyValidator.validate`, driven once per arrangement from a throwaway JUnit case over `DrugReferenceTestSupport.shippedEntries()`, at the shipped severity floor (`minor`):

- The 72 above-floor rules Decision 39 recorded reproduce exactly under the ticket's own predicate. Of those, **62 raise a folded chip** in the arrangement measured (the patient on the partner row's own name and codes, asked about the subject).
- **Before: 0 of the 62 named their one active order once. After: 28.** The tokens that move are `ketoconazole`, `dexfenfluramine`, `dextroamphetamine` and `atropine`; the ones that do not are `penicillin g`, `antithrombin iii` and `gabapentin`.
- The first two of those are ties: no substance claims `penicillin g` or `antithrombin iii` as its own display name, so nothing says which of them a rule is about. `gabapentin` is not a tie — `Gabapentin` claims it outright — but `canonicalRow` resolves the shared code to `Gabapentin enacarbil`, whose claim is an alias, so the ladder's own row loses. That is the row-own-claim rule above doing what it is for.

Re-derive rather than trusting the figures; they are a property of the dataset and of the arrangement measured, not of this code.

### Trade-offs

- **− The ranking decides which substance a token NAMES, not which row a rule was AUTHORED on.** `ddinter` derives a rule's token from the partner row's `rxnorm_name`, so a rule authored on `Levoketoconazole` carries the token `ketoconazole` too, and the reconciled chip prints its prose under `Ketoconazole`. A subject can carry two above-floor rules under that one token: `Osilodrostat` carries a `Major` rule against each of `Ketoconazole` and `Levoketoconazole`, and both rows publish `rxnorm_name: ketoconazole` (the two rows, their four shared ATC codes and both mechanism texts are in `ddi-fold-outranked-token.json`, copied field-for-field from the shipped file). Nothing NEW is asserted — the unfolded chip already printed that same token — but the prose can name the other row. Closing it means choosing between two rules, which is `bestRulePerPartner`'s question rather than this one.
- **− The ranking needs the alias list stored TRIMMED, which is a change outside the fold.** `nameMatchStrength` gates on `matchesDrugName`, which trims neither operand, while `isNamed` trims both — so an alias carrying padding IS one of the entry's names by the gate and matches nothing by the rank, and both directions of that are wrong here. Measured through the real `JsonDrugReferenceSource` and the real `validate`: a curated entry named only by `" warfarin"` lost a reconciliation the existence form had made (`Acenocoumarol interacts with active order Warfarin … as active order Warfarin` before, `… active order warfarin … as active order Warfarin` after), and the same padding on a RIVAL row dropped that row out of the contest and licensed the displacement — one substance's rated mechanism under another's name, which is the failure the ranking exists to prevent. Closed at `DrugReference.setAliases`, the one place every source writes the list, rather than in either predicate: the disagreement is a property of the stored string. Exhaustive for it rather than a patch on one shape — searched over both operands across space, tab, NBSP, NUL, precomposed and decomposed accents, dotted capital I, hyphen, period and digits, every pair where `isNamed` holds and the rank falls below `NAME_IS_ANOTHER_NAME` has an alias differing from its own `trim()`, the other being an alias folding to empty, which the loader already drops. No bundled dataset changes: 0 padded aliases over the shipped KB, the curated seed and the DDInter excerpt. Pinned by `aPaddedAliasNamesTheOneOrderOnce`, which reddens if the trim is removed.
- **+ A CONTESTED token is admissible only at `NAME_IS_THE_DISPLAY_NAME`, which is what bounds this change downstream.** Every rival reaching the comparison passed `isNamed`, so the token IS one of that rival's stored aliases — and on a loaded dataset identity then implies containment (those aliases are stored trimmed, below, and the loader leaves none that names nothing), so the rival ranks at least `NAME_IS_ANOTHER_NAME`. Only the top rank strictly outranks that. Measured over the shipped KB: of the 18 contested pairs the predicate admits, 18 are at the top rank and none has a `displayLabel` differing from the token by anything but case — so all 28 reconciliations above are case-only (`ketoconazole`→`Ketoconazole`, `atropine`→`Atropine`, and so on). The prompt's name union for that partner therefore cannot move, and `SubjectRule.partnerKey` case-folds to the label the chip now renders, so #121's key-versus-name gap is not widened by any case this decision adds. The UNCONTESTED path is untouched and still admits at either rank. **Two surfaces, not one, since [Decision 51](#decision-51-the-injected-record-names-a-folded-chips-partner-in-its-own-vocabulary) landed first**: the ENTRY rung of `foldedPartnerLabel` also hands the injected `drug_reference` note `labelEntry.getName()` under this same gate, so this widening moves that prompt text too. There the bound is a derivation rather than the measurement above — the top rank IS `normalizeName(token).equals(normalizeName(name))`, and both that name and `partnerLabel`'s token reach the note trimmed, so a contested token's note name is that token re-cased. The measurement is needed only for the chip, whose `displayLabel()` can append a generic the rank says nothing about.
- **− 34 of the 62 folded chips still show Decision 39's symptom**, and they are not all ties. 20 (`penicillin g`) and 12 (`antithrombin iii`) are: no substance claims those tokens as its own display name, so nothing says which of them a rule is about, and admitting on a tie would be the `Omeprazole`/`Esomeprazole` displacement in a different spelling. The other 2 are `gabapentin`, where the ranking is decisive and the ladder still loses because `canonicalRow` hands it the weaker claimant — recorded here rather than left to read as a tie, because closing it is a question about `entryForAtcCode`'s pick and not about this predicate. That shape is not gabapentin's alone: `A02BC05` resolves to `Omeprazole` against the token `esomeprazole` the same way, and there refusing is exactly what the guard is for. What is named here is the instance among these 62 chips, not the extent of the shape.
- **− `uniqueStrongestClaimant` is now asked of a rule TOKEN as well as of a name the chart recorded**, which is wider than `DrugReference.nameMatchStrength`'s stated scope. The alternative was a second spelling of "strictly outranks every rival" inside the validator, and a second spelling could drift from this one in the direction that matters — admitting where this refuses puts one substance's rated mechanism under another's name. The candidate sets stay the callers' own and differ deliberately; only the comparison is shared.
- **+ The two directions #292 pinned both still hold, and now for reasons that distinguish them.** `aRuleWhoseTokenNamesTwoSubstancesKeepsItsOwnToken` refuses because the ladder's `Omeprazole` row claims `esomeprazole` only as an alias while the `Esomeprazole` row claims it outright; `aRuleAboutAnotherSubstanceSharingTheCodeKeepsItsOwnToken` refuses at the `isNamed` gate, unchanged.
## Decision 53: The ANSWER no longer decides what a substance is called, so the two safety passes stop disagreeing

**Status: Accepted** (August 2026) — implemented, issue [#238](https://github.com/openmrs/openmrs-module-chartsearchai/issues/238) item 1. Changes which reference ROW a chip and an injected finding name a substance by, and — as a consequence the ticket did not name — which of a substance's published dose ceilings a dose chip quotes. Raises and suppresses no chip; the wire shape is unchanged.

### Context

`DrugSafetyValidator.SubstanceSubjects` is issue [#206](https://github.com/openmrs/openmrs-module-chartsearchai/issues/206)'s fix: the three chip arms that name a subject — the drug-in-play interaction chip, the dose warning and the contraindication chip — share ONE answer to "which row does this response call this substance by", so one response cannot call one substance three things.

`validate` runs **twice** per `/search`. The pre-answer pass reaches it through `DrugReferenceInjector.preAnswerFindings`, with an EMPTY answer, and each warning it raises is rendered as a citable `safety_finding` record in the prompt the model reads. The chips pass runs after the answer, and its `inPlay` set additionally carries the rows the ANSWER's own wording resolved (issue [#175](https://github.com/openmrs/openmrs-module-chartsearchai/issues/175) admits them deliberately, so that an arm never chooses its rule from a smaller candidate set than the screening arm would).

So the subject was decided per PASS. Where the answer resolved a row of an in-play substance the question did not, the group grew, the fold answered differently, and the citable record said one name while the chip beside the answer said another. `SubstanceSubjects`' own javadoc recorded this, called it "reasoned rather than measured", and rejected the obvious remedy: *"the alternative is worse: naming from a pass-invariant set while RULING from the whole group is the two-row-sets shape this class exists to remove, one level up. Closing it properly means deciding a substance's subject once per REQUEST rather than once per pass, which is the injector's and the inference service's business rather than this class's."*

### Decision

Name from the rows the ANSWER cannot move; rule from all of them. `validate` builds a second group map, `namingRows = resolvedSubstanceRows(questionDrugs, orderEntries)` — the rows the question resolved and the rows the patient's own active orders resolved — and `SubstanceSubjects` folds THAT, falling back to the full group for a substance no naming row reaches. Every arm still reaches its rules and its bands over `resolvedRows`, unchanged.

**Both halves of the rejection above are wrong, and each for its own reason.**

*"The injector's and the inference service's business."* No. Of every input the naming decision reads, exactly one varied between the passes from INSIDE `validate` — `answer`. The question is the same string at both sites; `orderEntries` is `findForActiveOrders(rawContext)`, which reads the order's ATC codes and `getActiveDrugNames()`, neither of which `withReferenceNames` touches (it writes only `activeDrugReferenceNames`); and `recordedDrugNames(context)` is `getActiveDrugNames()`. So the pass-varying input is removed *inside* `validate`, with nothing carried between the passes and no new parameter on the public entry point. That is narrower than "the two passes cannot disagree" — see the trade-off below, which is the honest bound. (`mappings` varies too — null in the pre-answer pass — but its only readers are the answer-side echo test and `SubjectMatter`'s cited-text corpus, so it reaches none of the three inputs the fold takes.) Invariance is also stronger than transport: a third caller of `validate` gets the property for free, where a carrier would have to be threaded to it.

*"The two-row-sets shape this class exists to remove."* No. The shape issues [#162](https://github.com/openmrs/openmrs-module-chartsearchai/issues/162), [#174](https://github.com/openmrs/openmrs-module-chartsearchai/issues/174) and #175 removed is arms **ruling** from different row sets, whose harm is a milder rule surviving where a more severe one existed — issue [#86](https://github.com/openmrs/openmrs-module-chartsearchai/issues/86)'s direction. No arm's candidate set moves here. And naming from a set narrower than the one that decides content is *already* this module's design: `interactionSubject(rows, context)`'s javadoc says of its out-of-class caller that it asks "over the union of `findImpliedByQuery`'s rows and every row the patient's orders resolve — deliberately NOT the narrower set it decides to INJECT … what a substance is CALLED may not depend on them." `namingRows` **is** that union.

**The direction is forced rather than chosen.** The pre-answer record is in the prompt the model has already read by the time an answer exists, so it cannot be renamed. Either the chip adopts the answer-blind name, or the two disagree. There is no third option in which both surfaces see the answer.

Two alternatives were weighed and are not this.

* **Carry the pre-answer pass's decision forward** — the javadoc's own proposal. It needs a cross-pass carrier: a parameter on the public `validate`, or safety-layer state on `PatientChart` (a prompt artefact). A field on the validator is forbidden outright, `CLAUDE.md`'s #172 rule — the bean is a Spring singleton. And it buys a weaker property: agreement because one pass transported an answer, rather than because neither can compute a different one.
* **Move only the arms where the disagreement is observable.** The dose arm cannot fire in the pre-answer pass (it parses a dose out of an empty answer), so it never disagrees across passes and does not, on its own, need the answer-blind subject. Leaving it behind would have pass 2 name one substance two ways — #206 re-created inside one pass. The three arms move together or not at all, which is what makes the dose-arm consequence below unavoidable rather than incidental.

### Consequences

- **+** **The citable record and the chip beside the answer key on one name.** The injected finding's `resourceUuid` is `ChartSearchAiUtils.resourceKey(type, drug)`, so `PerRequestSubstanceSubjectTest` asserts the equality on the key rather than on prose. A containment assertion could not: `Estrone sulfate` is a prefix of `Estrone sulfate (topical)`, so the very divergence being fixed would satisfy one.
- **+** **The property is structural, so it holds for callers that do not exist yet — for a substance `namingRows` reaches at all.** `namingRows` is a function of the question and the patient's orders; any future third `validate` pass agrees with the other two without being told to, for such a substance. Not universal: a substance in play only because ONE pass's own answer named it has no group in `namingRows`, and `SubstanceSubjects.groupOf` then folds that pass's own answer-widened `allGroups` instead — so two passes fed two different answers can still name that substance differently, and a third caller inherits that exception along with the invariant. Demonstrated over this change's own `drug-reference-answer-named-substance-row.json`: a question naming neither Estrone row, answered `"Estrone can be given."` versus `"Estrone sulfate can be given."`, names the chip `Estrone sulfate (topical)` in one case and `Estrone sulfate` in the other. `groupOf`'s own javadoc states this narrower claim correctly; this bullet used to overclaim past it, as did `SubstanceSubjects`' class javadoc.
- **−** **The dose arm's quoted ceiling moves with the name, and this is a real trade.** `addOverdose` tries the SUBJECT's own band first — its javadoc: "the row tried first has followed that choice ever since [#206]" — so over a fixture publishing 3000 mg/day for a route-qualified row and 2000 for its unqualified sibling, a stated 4000 mg/day is now reported against the named row's own 3000 where it was reported against the answer-widened subject's 2000. The laxer figure. Three things bound it, all of them that arm's own recorded decisions: **no warning is lost**, because the walk after the subject still reaches every row (measured at 2500 mg/day, which clears the named row's band: the warning is raised against the sibling's 2000, carrying `ceilingAttribution`'s clause saying whose ceiling it is); the order was **already** first-to-trip and not strictest, stated under issue [#245](https://github.com/openmrs/openmrs-module-chartsearchai/issues/245); and reporting two ceilings for one substance is what the #174 collapse deliberately gave up, "nothing here knows which formulation is in play … so a second ceiling is a second guess, not a second fact". Both directions are pinned rather than stated.
- **−** **An existing chip can move where the answer matched a row the patient's orders also carry, with no answer-ONLY row involved.** `resolvedSubstanceRows` builds a group from `inPlay` first and appends order rows after, so such a row sits at its answer position in `resolvedRows` and at its `orderEntries` position in `namingRows` — the same objects in a different ORDER. `DrugReference.canonicalRow` keeps the incumbent on a full tie, so for a family tying on both of its rungs the group's order decides. Nothing in the suite reddened, so this is stated as reachable rather than as observed — no shipped or fixture family was found in that state, and no count over the knowledge base is offered for it.
- **+** **The two PAIR arms are covered too, closing [Decision 49](#decision-49-one-name-per-substance-per-pass-at-the-cost-of-the-question-pair-arms-pass-stability)'s own accepted cost.** This entry was drafted against a `main` where `addQuestionPairInteractions` and `addActiveOrderPairInteractions` still resolved their subjects through a private `canonicalSubjects`, over that arm's own rows — the state Decision 49 costed. Issue #236 merged ahead of this change and deleted `canonicalSubjects`, moving both pair arms onto the shared `SubstanceSubjects` lookup this entry also folds. Because that lookup is what `namingRows` now feeds, and because every substance either pair arm asks about is one of `questionDrugs` or an order-resolved row — both of which `namingRows` is built from directly — neither arm's naming decision can fall through to the answer-widened fallback. **The screening arm's version of this is stronger than "cannot fall through": it is not merely covered, it cannot diverge.** `addActiveOrderPairInteractions` has exactly one call site, gated on `questionDrugs.isEmpty()`, so for that arm `namingRows = resolvedSubstanceRows(questionDrugs, orderEntries)` reduces to `resolvedSubstanceRows(<empty set>, orderEntries)` — `substanceRows(<empty set>)` is an empty map, so the fold is nothing but "walk `orderEntries` and `groupFor`/`add` each row", which is byte-for-byte the `substances = substanceRows(orderDrugs)` map the arm builds for itself a few lines above: same membership, same order. Every row the arm ever hands `subjectOf` is drawn from that same list, so the lookup and the arm's own construction are not two things that happen to agree, they are one construction reached two ways. Recorded where the arm calls `subjectOf`, in `DrugSafetyValidator`. The question-pair arm's guarantee is the weaker "cannot fall through" one above: `namingRows` also carries order rows the arm's own pair loop never sees, so the two constructions are not provably identical there, only provably non-empty for the rows this arm asks about. Decision 49's own reproducer (`Does Daxibotulinumtoxina interact with kanamycin?`, driven with each pass's own answer) now prints the identical `Kanamycin interacts with Daxibotulinumtoxina (botulinum toxin type a), also named in the question` in both passes; reverting this change's own constructor call back to `new SubstanceSubjects(resolvedRows, resolvedRows, …)` reproduces Decision 49's original divergence. `PerRequestSubstanceSubjectTest` pins this for the question-pair arm.
- **−** **The injector's inputs now coincide with the chip layer's, and that must not be read as agreement.** `DrugReferenceInjector.SubstanceRendering.rows` is the same union `namingRows` is, so the two can no longer diverge over an answer-only row — but `collect` keys on `substanceKey()` falling back to the row's own id where `groupFor` falls back to `substanceGroupKey()`, and the production comment beside it names claiming otherwise as the hazard. The narrow claim is the one recorded.
- **−** **Invariance inside `validate` is not invariance across the request, and the first draft of this entry claimed it was.** Each pass builds its own `PatientClinicalContext` from the patient — the injector at its own entry point, the chips pass at `validate(String, String, Patient, List)` — so `orderEntries` and the recorded names are one function of TWO chart reads separated by the LLM call (149–154 s on the demo). Either can move the naming answer again: `PatientClinicalContextBuilder`'s catch wraps the whole order loop, so a failed active-order read degrades to an empty or a silently PARTIAL set, with no flag (unlike `contraindicationRecordsRead`, which #208 gave one for exactly this reason), and `getActiveOrders` is as-of-now, so an order lapsing mid-request is absent from the second read. Not closed, and deliberately not closed here: a divergent order read already changes which chips exist at all rather than only what they are called, so a carrier for the NAME would fix the smaller half of that. `PerRequestSubstanceSubjectTest` cannot see it — each of its cases builds one context object and hands it to both passes (most via `DrugReferenceTestSupport.contextNaming`, the question-pair-arm case via a plain `ctx` call).
- **−** **The reachability example this ticket inherited does not hold on shipped data.** `SubstanceSubjects`' javadoc named `Estrone sulfate (topical)` as a family whose rows publish different alias sets, on the strength of its publishing the bare `estrone`. Measured through the real `DdiDrugReferenceSource.parse` of the shipped knowledge base: its unqualified sibling publishes `estrone` too, because the `ddinter` parser builds both rows' aliases from one `rxnorm_name`, and `findImpliedByQuery` on a question saying `estrone` resolves a different substance entirely. So the divergence wants a curated `sourceFormat=json` dataset — which is what the new fixture is — or a `ddinter` family whose `rxnorm_name` is not word-bounded inside its display name. Corrected in the javadoc rather than left as evidence for a claim it does not support.
- **−** **The defect was never measured live and still has not been.** No count over the shipped knowledge base is published here: it needs a family whose rows publish different alias sets, which the module's own data statements say is not the usual shape, and the property being bought — that the two passes stop disagreeing over `answer` — does not depend on how often they would otherwise have differed. A frequency would be a nice-to-have; asserting one without measuring it through the real predicates is what `CLAUDE.md` forbids.


## Decision 54: The patient's co-medications are resolved once per validate pass, and the pairwise cap is not the lever

**Status: Accepted** (August 2026) — implemented, issue [#256](https://github.com/openmrs/openmrs-module-chartsearchai/issues/256).

### Context

#256 reports that `DrugSafetyValidator.validate` grows steeply with the number of drugs a question puts in play (D), attributes the growth to the pairwise arms, and observes that `chartsearchai.drugSafety.maxPairChips` (#131) caps CHIPS rather than WORK. It asks for the relayed figures to be re-derived, and poses a discriminator: *can the pairwise arms stop enumerating once the cap is reached, or does the cap's ordering requirement force full enumeration?* Its own provenance line says the numbers were relayed and not re-derived.

Re-derived through the real `validate` — real `DdiDrugReferenceSource` load of the shipped 19 MB knowledge base, the real curated cross-reactivity groups, a context of 43 active orders with 16 carrying ATC codes and 27 by name only (the 3.7.1 ratio), 5 warmups and 20 reps, runs alternated between the two heads. Before the change:

| drugs in play | 43 active orders | no active orders |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 95–130 ms | 1.2 ms |
| 2 | 136–188 ms | 3.7–4.6 ms |
| 5 | 224–307 ms | 9.6–11.7 ms |
| 10 | 482–488 ms | 30.3 ms |

**The attribution in the ticket is wrong.** The per-extra-drug cost scales with the number of ORDERS, not with D — 2.15 ms at 0 orders, 5.12 at 5, 17.70 at 20, 35.04 at 43 — so the dominant term is D × O, a product, not D². The pairwise arms sit inside the "no active orders" column, which is everything the chart does not drive — those arms plus the drug-in-play ones — and that column is about 30 ms at ten drugs in play, roughly 6% of the pass. So 30 ms bounds the arms the ticket blamed rather than measuring them. A probe wrapping `orderPartners` (one run, not interleaved) attributed 42% of the one-drug pass and 77% of the ten-drug pass to that one method, at 1, 4 and 7 calls for D = 1, 5 and 10.

`DrugSafetyValidator.classRelationships` runs per in-play SUBSTANCE and called `orderPartners(context)` each time. That resolution is a function of the pass's `context` and the loaded dataset, both fixed for the pass. The method's own javadoc had already named the remedy and declined to build it — *"widening them to the whole `validate` pass would … cut the repeated full scans, but they must then be locals threaded through the pass and NEVER fields"*, and *"the per-PASS memo stays unbuilt … Measure it the same way, interleaved and more than once, before treating the repeat as free at a larger order count."* #256 is that measurement.

### Decision

**One lazy per-pass resolution, threaded as a local: `DrugSafetyValidator.CoMedications`.**

- `validate` constructs one and hands it to `addInteractionWarnings` → `classRelationships` / `ruleAbout`. `orderPartners` has one caller, inside the memo.
- **Lazy, not eager.** A question that puts no substance in play — the commonest one — resolves nothing at all today, because `classRelationships` is never reached. An eager hoist into `validate` would make that question pay for a chart nothing asks about.
- **Two memos, because there are two per-subject sweeps and memoising the resolution does not reach the second.** `ruleAbout` calls the uncached sweep — a full walk of `getAll()` — once per (subject, partner, code), so `entryByCode` is held by the memo for the whole pass and passed into `orderPartners` as a parameter rather than declared as its local.
- **The sweep is named apart from the memo (`sweepForAtcCode` against `entryForAtcCode(String, Map)`), and that is a guard decision rather than a naming preference.** The source scan forbids a NAME inside a whitelist of bodies. While the two were overloads of one name, that whitelist had to permit the name wherever the cache is passed — inside `orderPartners`, inside `soleSubstanceOf` and inside `CoMedications.entryForCode` — so dropping the `cache` argument at any of them reinstated the full walk per (subject, partner, code) as an *overload resolution*, not as a new mention: measured in review at both sites, that mutation left `CoMedicationResolutionPerPassTest` and the whole api suite green. No needle over a name could have seen it, and the shape is no longer measurable because it no longer compiles. Under two names it does not compile, and a call written to the sweep's own name is a mention outside its two permitted bodies (its own, and the memoising overload's).
- **A per-call LOCAL and never a field**, #172's rule — and which of that rule's two reasons applies is asked per memo rather than recited, because they do not both bind. The bean is a Spring singleton, so a field would be one unsynchronized structure shared by every concurrent request: that binds both. The partner list additionally has no key at all — it is this patient's chart — so a field would answer for whoever asked first. `entryByCode` is bounded by the ATC code space and its values are a function of the loaded dataset rather than of any chart, so it takes the singleton reason alone.

**The ticket's discriminator is answered NO, and recorded on `maxPairChips()` itself** so the misreading is not re-derived from the cap's name: every candidate pair must be enumerated and evaluated before the cut, because the cut is defined as "the least severe go" and a pair's rating is not known until it has been evaluated — an early stop would change WHICH pairs are dropped, which is the one property the cap exists to guarantee (#131).

### Alternatives rejected

- **Stop the pairwise arms enumerating at the cap** — the ticket's own proposal. Refused on the ordering requirement above, and moot on the measurement: it could recover at most the 30 ms a chart-less pass costs at ten drugs — itself an upper bound, since the drug-in-play arms are inside it — leaving the ~460 ms that actually grew untouched.
- **Widen `orderPartners`' four internal per-call memos to the pass, which is what its javadoc literally suggested.** Memoising the RESULT is strictly stronger — it removes the walk, the `OrderPartner` construction and `codesForThisSubstancesPresentations` as well as the sweeps — and simpler, one local rather than four threaded maps. The one thing the result memo does NOT reach is `ruleAbout`, which is why `entryByCode` is threaded as well; that half of the javadoc's suggestion is taken.
- **Leaving `ruleAbout`'s sweep alone as immaterial.** Measured small: 0, 0, 3 and 5 sweeps at one, two, five and ten drugs in play, against 93 to 433 dataset-walk calls in total, over a chart built so that every active order shares an ATC subgroup with an in-play drug — because `ruleAbout` returns before resolving anything when the subject has no rule about an active order, and a class-only chip is the ordinary outcome. Kept anyway: it is a second copy of the same defect, invisible to any fixture that does not reach it, and the invariant the test pins would otherwise be fixture-conditional.
- **A behavioural test for a field REASSIGNED once per pass.** There is none to write: such a field sweeps exactly as often as a local does, which is the limit `CLAUDE.md` already records for the analogous `recordedAllergens` memo. Scope that claim to the reassigned shape — the OTHER field shape, initialised lazily and never reassigned, is caught behaviourally too, and by cases that have nothing to do with this change — the second patient of a run reads the first one's chart, so `FoldedChipOnePartnerNameTest` reddens among others. Mutate it and read the failures rather than trusting a count here: one stood in this sentence and went stale within four commits, the reflective guard having joined the cases that catch it. The reassigned shape is what the source scan is for, and it asserts the class's whole FIELD BUDGET — one mutable field, the injected service — rather than looking for the memo's own type: that half is asked of the compiled CLASS (`getDeclaredFields`) rather than of the source text, and that is the settlement of an arms race rather than a preference. As a regex over `DrugSafetyValidator.java` it was defeated in review again and again, each fix opening the next and every one green across the whole suite: a memo typed as something other than `CoMedications`; that field given a parenthesised INITIALISER, which met an exclusion written for method declarations; and the declaration prefixed with `@SuppressWarnings("…")`, whose own paren the match-start could not cross. Each is a way of WRITING a field, and a reader of text can always be written around. Reflection sees the field however it is spelled — and additionally catches a non-final STATIC, which the textual version exempted along with the class's many constants. **A `static final` field is collected too where it HOLDS a container**, because `final` binds the reference and not the contents: `private static final Map<PatientClinicalContext, List<OrderPartner>> CACHE = new LinkedHashMap<>()` is the most idiomatic way a Java author writes a memo and is #172's trap in its worst form — unsynchronized on a singleton, keyed on a per-request object so it grows for the life of the JVM — and under a modifier check alone it was exempt: added to the class with `CoMedications.resolved()` reading and populating it, the whole api suite stayed green and the behavioural cases here could not see it either, the memo still resolving once per pass. The rule asks the declared type and, failing that, the value, so a memo declared as `Object` is caught as well, and it false-positives on nothing today — the class's constants are a `Logger`, `Pattern`s and `int`s. What it cannot see is state held in ANOTHER class or a `ThreadLocal`; both were tried and both fail the suite catastrophically, Surefire running one JVM in which the leak reaches unrelated test classes. The residue on this class is a mutable object that is neither a container nor declared as one — a bespoke holder type — which no type-shaped rule reaches.

### Why sharing one partner list across subjects is sound

Read out of the source rather than assumed. Every write to an `OrderPartner` — `nameByOrder`, `codes.add`, `codes.addAll`, `substances.add`, `substances.addAll`, `codesFromDataset` — happens inside `orderPartners` / `addPartnersForUnmappedOrders` while the list is being built; every touch downstream is a read (`classRelationships`, `ruleAbout`, `foldedPartnerLabel`, the chip sentences). `OrderPartner` declares no `equals`/`hashCode`, and the only structure keyed on one is the `LinkedHashMap` `classRelationships` builds fresh per call and its caller consumes inside one loop. `PatientClinicalContext` exposes no mutator, so the pass's context cannot change under the memo. And the two collection consumers copy rather than mutate: `DrugReference.atcSubgroups(Set)` builds a new set, `CrossReactivityGroup.containsAnyCode` only iterates.

That was already true before this change and nothing pinned it, because until now it cost little: each in-play substance got a freshly built list, so a stray write reached that subject and no other. Sharing the list is what makes it load-bearing — one write now reaches every later subject of the pass and the citable findings the injector renders from them — so it is pinned, structurally, by `CoMedicationResolutionPerPassTest.onlyTheTwoBuildersWriteToACoMedication`. Qualified writes only: `OrderPartner`'s own unqualified writes to `label`/`namingOrder`/`namesADrug` are Decision 40's rule with its own guard, and its collection fields are final, so what is left to forbid is a caller reaching in.

### Effect

Same measurement, alternated runs, 43 active orders: ten drugs in play 482–488 ms → 130–173 ms; per extra drug in play 39–40 ms → 3.4–4.4 ms, and nearly flat in the order count where it used to be linear in it: 2.51 / 2.64 / 2.88 / 3.76 ms per extra drug at 0 / 5 / 20 / 43 orders, against 2.15 / 5.12 / 17.70 / 35.04 before. The residue is about 1.3 ms per extra drug across 43 orders, and it is re-folding: a `validate` pass derives `foldedLower` of the same strings over and over, because `containsBoundedToken` folds both of its operands at the innermost comparison. **This paragraph used to attribute that residue to `PatientClinicalContext.hasActiveDrug` and to quote 251,788 `matchesOrderName` calls for it. Both halves were wrong and Decision 55 corrects them** (issue [#330](https://github.com/openmrs/openmrs-module-chartsearchai/issues/330)). The figure was measured on this change's PRE-fix branch, where `hasActiveDrug` ran 5,989 times in a ten-drug pass against 1,954 after it; and that arm was never the dominant folder even then — the dataset alias scan was, at roughly nine times its volume. Nobody counted the scan because the probe that produced the figure was a counter placed INSIDE `hasActiveDrug`, so it could only ever see that one caller. **That is the same mistake this decision's own "the attribution in the ticket is wrong" section is about, made one layer along and by the same instrument.** Left out of this change for a reason that still stands — the fix is in `PatientClinicalContext` and `DrugReference`, which this slice does not touch — but it was a follow-up about the wrong arm. One drug in play is indistinguishable, 95–130 ms against 96–134 ms, which is expected: one resolution either way.

Behaviour-neutral, verified rather than argued: the full rendered chip list — type, severity and detail in full, for questions naming one to five drugs against an eight-order chart, 89 chips — is byte-identical across the two heads, order included.

### Trade-offs

- **+** The growth the ticket reports is removed at its cause, and the cause is not the one the ticket names. What remains is bounded by the chart-less pass, 30 ms at ten drugs in play.
