- **+** `validate` runs twice per request, so the saving is paid twice.
- **−** Each pass still resolves the chart once, and `DrugReferenceInjector` resolves the active orders again for its own promotion predicate. Sharing one resolution across the two passes of a request is a larger change across the injector/validator boundary and is not taken here.
- **−** The pairwise arms still enumerate every pair. That is the ordering requirement, not an oversight, and it is now recorded where the cap is defined.
- **−** The structural guards read source text, so they stop a regression and not a determined author. Review pass after review pass defeated one, and every shape but the last was closed: a memo field typed as something other than `CoMedications`, that field given a parenthesised initialiser, the declaration prefixed with an annotation, the resolvers reached by method reference, a `static final` collection exempted by a modifier check, and the uncached sweep reached by dropping an overload's `cache` argument — all ways ordinary code gets written, so a regression can be made of them by accident. The last, reaching a resolver through `getDeclaredMethod("sweepForAtcCode", …)` with `setAccessible`, is left open: the scan blanks string literals by design (a method named in a javadoc is prose), and writing reflection into a private call inside the same class is a deliberate act rather than an accident. Closing it would need a bytecode or constant-pool check, and the next syntax after that would still be open. Named on the guard itself rather than left to be discovered.
- **−** The two older source-scanning copies (`ChipSubjectOneResolutionTest`, `OrderPartnerNameSourceWritePathTest`) are not migrated onto `SourceScan`, which this change extracted at the third caller — the threshold `ModuleSourceRoot`'s own javadoc records. They have already diverged from each other in their block-comment handling; migrating them is a change of its own, and the second keeps its file locator apart from `ModuleSourceRoot` for a reason its own javadoc states.

## Decision 56: The injected reference slice gets a durable observable, and deliberately no cap

**Status: Accepted** (August 2026) — implemented, issue [#229](https://github.com/openmrs/openmrs-module-chartsearchai/issues/229).

### Context

`DrugReferenceInjector` appends `drug_reference` and `safety_finding` records to the prompt. `MAX_INTERACTION_RENDER_CHARS` bounds ONE record's interaction prose; nothing bounds how many records there are, so N records cost N times it, and `LlmInferenceService` never checks the assembled length against the `-c <contextSize>` llama-server is started with.

#229 measured the reachable size through the real `injectRecords` over the shipped 19 MB `ddinter` knowledge base and the demo patients' real order sets — 24 patients x 21 questions = 504 cells, on a worktree of `main` and one of the #151 branch. Records 464 to 491 (+5.8%), characters 460,982 to 488,668 (+6.0%), 19 of 504 cells changed, chip count changed 0. Worst single cell, Sarah Taylor x prednisolone: **1 record / 903 chars to 6 records / 5183 chars**. The ticket calls that "roughly 4% of the 32,768-token default context" (`ChartSearchAiConstants.DEFAULT_LLM_CONTEXT_SIZE` verifies the 32,768); the conversion it does not state is about four characters to the token, which is the assumption that fraction rests on. Its own comment adds the other half: #228 makes the duplicate-therapy class arm reach every active order rather than the dictionary-classified subset, every chip is also injected as a citable `safety_finding` (#110), and `findings` is uncapped at every layer — worst cell 3 to 8 chips over the demo corpus, chips overall 554 to 577 across 888 cells, so the average is unchanged and it is the tail that moves. The two sweeps report different cell counts — 504 and 888 — because they are two fix passes' own measurements over their own corpora rather than one sweep read twice; each names its base, and nothing here reconciles them because nothing can. `maxPairChips` does not reach any of it: it caps the two PAIRWISE arms, and class chips were never capped (measured unmoved by #228, max chips/cell 10 to 10).

Both figures are RELAYED from those two fix passes and are not re-derived here; the ticket says so of the first and this decision says so of both.

**The observability half is the worse half, and it is the one that had no floor at all.** The only signal that any of this happened was one `log.debug` in `injectRecords`. On this instance that line is not reachable by configuration alone: the `log.level` global property is not applied at startup — setting it by SQL and restarting produced 0 DEBUG lines, while POSTing the same value to `/ws/rest/v1/systemsetting` applied it immediately and it was lost again on the next restart. That is openmrs-core's behaviour rather than this module's, but it was this module's only observable for prompt cost, so the size of the slice a clinician's answer was built from could not be established after the fact by any means.

### Decision

**Report it, per answer, on the audit row — and do not cap it.**

- **One derivation, keyed on the provenance classification.** `ChartSearchAiUtils.referenceSlice(List<RecordMapping>)` returns a record count and a character total for every mapping `referenceGroup` calls reference material. It reads the classification and never a type name, so a further injected reference kind is measured automatically — the same argument `isGroundingDemoteOnly`'s javadoc already makes for having no type list of its own. `isGroundingDemoteOnly` and the new derivation now share one private `isReferenceMaterial` delegate rather than spelling the comparison twice; the metric deliberately does not call `isGroundingDemoteOnly`, because that names a GRADING rule and a prompt-cost measurement must not depend on what grounding does.
- **The injector's DEBUG line prints the slice BESIDE the entry-only total it already printed, and does not replace it.** The first design deleted that private sum on the reasoning that the log and the row must state the same number. The build refuted it: `ReferenceRecordSubstanceCollapseTest.theDebugLineReportsTheDrugReferenceEntryCharacterTotalAndOnlyThat` is the specification for that line and asserts the total is the drug-reference ENTRIES' characters and explicitly not the findings' — because that number is issue #163's, one near-duplicate entry per route variant, and widening it would overstate what #163 measures. So they are two questions and the line now answers both, labelled. That is better than either alone: an operator correlating a log line with an audit row can see which population each number is, which is the thing the unification was trying to protect and would have achieved by making #163's number unreadable instead.
- **The producer states it; no consumer re-derives it.** `LlmInferenceService` resolves the slice once per method, off the chart `inject` returned — the chart the LLM sees — and sets it on every `ChartAnswer` that method produces, the ungrounded early-done one included. This is `search_mode`'s discipline (#178) and for the same reason: by the time the REST layer files a row, the chart is gone.
- **Null is not zero.** `ChartAnswer.getReferenceSlice()` returns null where the producer stated none, and `reference_slice_records` / `reference_slice_chars` are nullable. Zero is a real and common measurement — a question matching no reference entry — and collapsing the two would make an unmeasured row indistinguishable from a measured empty one.
- **`active_drug_order` records are outside the number.** They are the patient's own prescriptions and group as chart evidence under the same classification. Their count stays on the DEBUG line.

**And no cap.** #229 lists one — "a GP in the shape of `maxPairChips`" — and says of it "Not obviously a cap", recommending the report instead because "(1) is cheap and closes the part that is actually dangerous". Three things decide it beyond the ticket's own preference. A cap on records is a cap on EVIDENCE, and what it drops is the supporting reference record for a chip that still renders — which is #151's failure mode exactly: the two layers disagreed about which orders the patient had, silently and in one direction, and what reached the clinician was a Major chip whose supporting record was simply not in the prompt. The cut would also have to choose WHICH records to drop, and Decision 54 already records why that is not free for the pairwise arms — an early stop changes which pairs are dropped, which is the one property #131's cap exists to guarantee — while `render`'s own note says "This ordering is what makes the `MAX_INTERACTION_RENDER_CHARS` cut meaningful", so a second cut layered above it is choosing against an ordering built for a different cut. And the measurement does not demand one: the worst observed cell is about 4% of the default context, and the shape is bounded in practice by how many substances a patient's orders resolve to. What is unbounded is the RULE, and the honest remedy for an unbounded rule nobody can currently see is to make it visible first. A cap can be added later on evidence the observable produces; it cannot be un-added from a prompt that silently lost a safety record.

### Alternatives rejected

- **A counter on `GET /chartsearchai/drugreferencestatus`**, which #229 offers as the other half of option 1. That endpoint answers about the LOADED DATASET — one lazily-built object, held for the life of the bean — while slice size is per question. A per-request counter there would be mutable state on a Spring singleton, which is the first of the two reasons `CLAUDE.md`'s memoisation rule gives for never holding one on these beans, and it could only ever report an aggregate, which cannot answer "how large was the slice behind THIS answer".
- **A per-type breakdown in the durable row** — separate counts for entries and findings. Not rejected because it cannot be written: buckets keyed on `mapping.getResourceType()` and admitted by the same classification name no type literal, which is what the controller already does when it puts `referenceGroup(...)` on the wire. Rejected because the row is where the prompt COST is read, and the cost is the group's; the per-kind split already exists on the DEBUG line for whoever can set the level, and two more nullable columns on a table whose history is a history of columns going stale is a poor trade for a number nobody has asked a question of yet.
- **Editing `chartsearchai-002` rather than adding a changeset**, which the file header's consolidation paragraph appears to invite. The header describes the SECOND step of a lifecycle whose first step this file's own history shows: `input_tokens`/`output_tokens` arrived as `chartsearchai-005` in April 2026 and were folded into 002's create-time definition by the consolidation in May. Editing 002 now adds nothing on an instance that has already run it, while the hbm mapping declares the columns immediately — the insert fails, `saveAuditLog` catches and logs, and auditing stops with no error anywhere. Numbered 009 rather than 003 because ids 001 to 006 have existed in this file on the main line and 007/008 exist in unmerged branches. Reusing one is not a silent skip, which is what an earlier draft of this paragraph said: liquibase stores a checksum beside id + author + filename, so an id an instance holds an orphan row for raises `ValidationFailedException` and stops the module starting. Measured on liquibase 4.32.0 — the version `openmrs-api` puts on this module's classpath — against an instance built from the consolidation-era file, which leaves orphan rows for 001 and 003. Loud rather than silent, and worse; the conclusion is the same.

### Trade-offs

- **+** The prompt cost of the module's own material is readable per answer, in SQL and on `GET /chartsearchai/auditlog`, with no log level involved.
- **+** The log line carries both numbers, labelled, so the row's figure can be reconciled against #163's without either being mistaken for the other.
- **−** The slice is still unbounded. This decision makes it visible and explicitly declines to bound it; a future cap is a decision of its own, and should be taken on numbers this observable produces rather than on the relayed ones above.
- **−** On an answer-cache hit the row states the ORIGINAL request's slice, because `ChartSearchServiceRouter` returns the cached `ChartAnswer` unchanged. Truthful for one row — that is the prompt the answer was built from — and it means summing the column across rows over-reports prompt spend by every cache hit. Inherited from `search_mode`'s behaviour rather than chosen, and recorded on the accessor.
- **−** Zero says what the prompt carried and not why it carried it. Several unlike situations produce it: nothing matched; `drugReference.enabled` is off, which is the shipped default; some combination of `injectFromQuery`, `injectFromOrders` and `drugSafety.validateAnswers` is off, none of which silences it alone; `inject` threw and degraded to the unmodified chart; or the injector added only the patient's own active-order records. Each is a true statement about the prompt, so no third value is owed the way `contraindicationRecordsRead()` is owed one — a chart the module could not read is not a chart that records nothing, but a prompt that carried nothing did carry nothing. What it costs is a reading: a column of zeros is not evidence the corpus raises no reference material, and the injector's log is the only thing separating a swallowed failure from an honest miss. That list is the paths visible today and is deliberately not offered as complete; a draft of this decision put a count on it without checking one.
- **−** The number counts reference-group records whoever minted them, so a reference-group type passed through by querystore would be included. That is the honest reading for a cost figure — the bytes cost the same whoever wrote the line — but it means the column is not a count of what the injector did.
- **−** Two character totals now exist in the module — the group slice and the injector's entry-only one — and they answer different questions rather than approximating each other. Each has its own test; a reader replacing either with the other reports one population under the other's name, which is what the first draft of this change did.
- **−** Characters exclude the `[N] ` citation prefix and the newline the chart assembly adds, so the figure is a floor on bytes spent rather than an exact prompt length.
- **−** The liquibase changeset is pinned by nothing. Nothing in this suite reads `liquibase.xml`; the schema the api tests exercise is the one Hibernate derives, so a column present in the mapping and absent from the changeset is invisible — exactly as it already is for `search_mode` and `input_tokens`.
